# Soklet 4.0.0 remediation plan — October 4, 2026

## Round 2 follow-up

The October 8 review is assessed in
[Soklet 4.0 Round 2 review](SOKLET_4_0_ROUND2_REVIEW_2026-10-09.md),
including the approved two-callback streaming amendment, current fixes,
retained policies and validation status. Earlier completed slices below retain
their original scope and evidence; they are not immutable candidate acceptance.

## Recommendation

Finish the correctness and security work below before declaring 4.0.0 ready for publication. The review identifies real remaining implementation work. Prioritize shared root causes, with a small regression-driven change per slice, rather than treating its 201 catalog entries as 201 independent projects.

Most changes should stay inside existing implementations. A few items concern public contracts and need a separate API review before implementation; those are listed explicitly below.

**Status:** S1a–S1f, S2a–S2k, S3a–S3e, S4a–S4f, S5a–S5j, S6a–S6r, S7a–S7f, S8a–S8r and S9a–S9u implemented with local validation. The user approved the floating-point sum and consistently boxed/List histogram snapshot surface; S9c implements that contract below. The user approved the exact additive maintenance count/key surface; S9e implements it below. The user approved the exact HTTP stream terminal callback and immutable count/key surface; S9f implements it below. The user approved the exact public SSE handshake getter; S8q implements it below. The user approved the smaller graceful HTTP-feed state check; S8r implements it below. S10a completes the full core matrix, static analysis, affected consumer checks and published-snippet compilation. S10b completes the reviewed API/freeze reconciliation and finite fuzz regressions. S10c completes current-source modern/2025 conformance, HTTP session recovery and pinned TypeScript SDK interoperability. Immutable candidate validation remains pending. Work is on the current branches. The user owns commits and pushes; the agent does not stage changes unless requested.

## Basis and limits of this assessment

- Input: `../../SOKLET_4_0_DEEP_REVIEW_2026-10-04.md`, including its verification corrections, deliberate decisions and coverage limitations.
- The report's runtime probes used core commit `02aa8522`. This assessment inspected core **`8e1c689b`**, which includes the subsequently committed List migration and strict text decoding. The working tree was clean when inspection began.
- I inspected the leading mechanisms in current core, servlet and OTel source, and independently ran small parsing and JVM carrier probes. I did **not** rerun the review's complete test suite, large subscription fan-out tests, heap-amplification tests, client-host sessions or every one of its 201 findings. Its probe results remain reviewer evidence unless identified below as independently reproduced.
- The report is useful evidence, not an authority to change settled API contracts. Each planned code fix first gets a bounded reproducer against current source. A corrected or obsolete finding is closed with its reason instead of receiving a speculative patch.
- The last green suite predates these proposed fixes. No candidate-validation or publication claim follows from this plan.

### Independent checks before implementation (`8e1c689b`)

| Finding | What was checked | Assessment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F257 | `Utilities.extractPathFromUrl` still passes leading `//` directly to `URI`. A bounded probe of the current compiled classes returns `/admin` for both raw and decoded `//x/admin?y=1`. | Confirmed path corruption. The ACL-bypass impact depends on intermediary normalization and policy configuration. Fix before release. |
| F258 | The raw-form helper still creates a synthetic URI. Probe: `a=one#two&b=three` becomes only `{a=[one]}`; a literal space causes `IllegalRequestException`. | Independently reproduced; strict UTF-8 and Lists did not fix it. |
| F259 | Probe: `text/plain; profile=test; charset=ISO-8859-1` produces no charset. | Independently reproduced; incorrect charset selection remains despite strict decoding. |
| F256 | Physical headers still run through `Utilities.addParsedHeader`, which splits selected comma-list fields; map-backed values do not follow the same path. | Current source confirms the production/simulator mismatch. |
| F149 | `normalizeLegacySessionRpcResponse` still returns early when `legacySessionSelected` is false. | Source confirms the normalization gap; the report's VS Code/SDK consequences were not independently replayed. |
| F029 | MCP's Host authorization still compares every supplied port to the listener's port before checking the allowlist. Configured allowed hosts cannot contain ports. | Source confirms the reverse-proxy obstruction. |
| F024 | URI-grant work increments session physical references; release updates `quiescentNanos`; idle expiry requires physical references to be zero and the quiescence interval to have elapsed. | Source confirms the problematic activity accounting. The report's default-duration lockout was not rerun. |
| F045 | The shared projection scheduler still has at most four workers and 128 pending jobs. | Capacity mismatch confirmed in source; precise loss counts remain reviewer evidence. |
| F169 | Fix/docs — S2b implemented; custom queued executors remain application-owned | Source confirms that execution can be queued after lifecycle reservation. |
| F194 | Fix — S2e implemented | Source supports the missed-finalization/span-leak mechanism. End-to-end regression required. |
| C003 | Fix — S2a implemented | JVM mechanism independently reproduced; the full 256-client Soklet incident was not rerun. |
| F068 / F195 | Pending-response and streaming EOF paths deliberately record `inputHalfClosed`, disable reads, and continue response delivery. | Mechanism confirmed. A blanket EOF-to-cancel patch would break legitimate HTTP half-close/pipelining. Scope the change by transport and protocol. |
| F067 | Fix — S2d implemented | Source confirms callback isolation needs work. |
| F074 / F092 | Elicitation numbers are routed through integer validation; typed integral conversion calls `BigDecimal.toBigIntegerExact` without an expansion-budget check at that boundary. | Source supports both findings. Avoid reproducing heap amplification on the user's workstation. |
| F273 / C016 | Static-file no-follow resolution passes the request-spelled path onward. Both servlet adapters have their own leftmost forwarded-client parsing. | Source confirms the risky mechanisms; filesystem alias and trust-chain regressions required. |
| F225 / F293 | `awaitShutdown()` only waits. The website still calls its removed trigger overload and recommends plain waiting for containers. | Confirmed migration/docs defect; preserve explicit lifecycle ownership. |

The carrier result is consistent with [OpenJDK's virtual-thread description](https://openjdk.org/jeps/444) and the [monitor implementation work](https://mail.openjdk.org/pipermail/core-libs-dev/2024-November/134311.html). On JDK 21 the `Object.wait` problem involves captured carriers and bounded scheduler compensation; “every synchronized block pins” would be an inaccurate diagnosis.

### Corrections to the review's suggested direction

1. **Disconnect behavior must be revision-specific.** Modern MCP says closing the SSE response stream cancels the request. The 2025 transport says disconnection should not itself mean cancellation. Preserve the latter and ordinary HTTP half-close behavior. See [modern cancellation](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/transports/streamable-http#cancellation) and [2025 transport rules](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports#sending-messages-to-the-server).
2. **F225 is an urgent migration correction, not a reason to restore implicit hooks inside `awaitShutdown()`.** Use `SokletApplication` for a standalone process; a manually owned JVM hook must call blocking `close()` or join shutdown completion.
3. **F228 is principally a documentation correction.** Asynchronous, observational transition callbacks are an existing decision. Essential flushing belongs in bounded `ShutdownCleanup`; arbitrary observer code must not hold shutdown hostage.
4. **F274's client-IP allegation was narrowed by the report's own verifier.** Its trust policy requires the edge to sanitize forwarded families. Retain that requirement; independently evaluate the effective-origin selection and adapter divergence. Choosing X-Forwarded-For merely because it exists does not establish trust.
5. **F273's check-then-`toRealPath()` suggestion is incomplete.** A pathname can change between the symlink check and the open. Preserve the no-follow and root-containment guarantees while resolving aliases; do not claim race safety from that check alone.
6. **C008 concerns an API tradeoff.** Automatically aborting every admitted HTTP stream at quiesce would truncate finite downloads. Correct misleading cooperation prose immediately, then review an explicit graceful-stop policy/signal.
7. **Some “low” entries merit release fixes.** Parsing differentials, typed-number expansion, authorization checks, synchronous-publisher recursion and an unusable SSE transport SPI are more important than cosmetic severity labels suggest.
8. **Appendix A needs two exceptions to blanket deferral.** Recheck F027 against the 2025 rule requiring a message to use only one connected stream; a documented duplicate-delivery design does not settle conformance. Recheck F286's absolute-form authority handling while fixing F257. See [2025 multiple connections](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports#multiple-connections) and [RFC 9112 request targets](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9112.html#section-3.2).

## Execution order

“Before release” below means fix a verified defect, or record a concrete, accurate disposition if its premise does not hold. It does not mean adding every suggested feature or configuration knob.

| Slice | Work | Exit condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | Request parsing, framing, file identity and proxy trust | No known routing/parser ambiguity or verified policy bypass remains. |
| S2 | Streaming execution, backpressure, finalization and transport feedback | Admission reflects actual producer capacity; slow readers and cancellation have bounded, correct outcomes. |
| S3 | MCP wire interoperability and proxy deployment | Both 2025 profiles work consistently with and without sessions; modern rules remain intact. |
| S4 | Legacy session and control-path reliability | Abandoned sessions expire, control traffic remains usable under load, and revocation is safe. |
| S5 | Subscription scheduling, authorization and event races | Healthy fan-out survives within advertised limits; authorization and change-delivery fences hold. |
| S6 | MCP application validation, state and bounded resource use | Malformed input is classified safely; input size cannot amplify into unbounded conversion work. |
| S7 | Routing and annotation-processor parity | Incremental builds and invalid declarations cannot create the reported runtime traps. |
| S8 | Lifecycle, SSE and simulation correctness | Shared deadlines, graceful/forced behavior, startup causes and simulator outcomes match their contracts. |
| S9 | Metrics/API decisions and user-facing documentation | Public contracts are reviewed; metrics are meaningful; published samples and migration instructions are current. |
| S10 | Final integration and release validation | Changed paths pass integration checks, then final candidate gates run from the user's final commits. |

### S1 — request parsing and security boundaries

Start with **S1a: F257, F258 and F259**. These are independently reproduced and can be addressed without expanding public API.

- Separate HTTP origin-form parsing from general URI authority parsing. Preserve every leading path component and the correct raw path, including repeated slashes. Verify ordinary absolute URLs, `OPTIONS *`, query extraction, dot-segment behavior, encoded-slash rejection and malformed UTF-8. Do not fix this by collapsing `//` into `/` or silently changing the decoded routing contract.
- Parse raw form pairs directly, rather than embedding form text into a URI. Preserve literal `#`, spaces and other legal form-value characters. Keep URL query validation distinct from form-body parsing.
- Parse Content-Type parameters with quote-aware rules, independent of ordering. Define duplicate/conflicting charset behavior and preserve strict charset decoding and fixed, redacted failures.
- Preserve duplicates and empty **values**. A bare `name` and `name=` remain one empty value; repeated empties remain multiple occurrences. Scalar binding continues to reject multiple occurrences before converting a single blank value to `Optional.empty()`.
- Proposed follow-up from the earlier parameter discussion: retain explicitly empty names (`=value`) under the empty-string key; distinguish them from separator-only gaps. Document the decision and test it. No new collection type or injection mode is needed.

Then split the remaining work into small changes:

- **Header occurrence model (F256):** recommend one List entry per physical field occurrence in every construction path. Keep comma parsing in field-specific consumers. A single `Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate, br` should be one scalar field value; two actual occurrences remain two and still fail singular injection. Audit the existing split-field consumers, both servlet bridges and compression/CORS/localization before changing the helpers. Preserve empty field values where the field grammar allows them; never deduplicate duplicate authentication or framing fields.
- **Framing (F178, F179, F284, F205, F216):** bound incremental parser scanning, validate chunk extensions, reject unsupported HTTP/1.0 transfer encoding, reject bare CR in SSE handshakes, and sanitize rejected SSE response framing. Use the same security rules across HTTP and SSE, including duplicate Content-Length and CL+TE rejection. RFC 9112 requires a bare CR to be invalid or replaced with SP, not treated as an independent line terminator: [message parsing](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9112.html#section-2.2).
- **Trusted proxy chain (C016, F274):** centralize adapter client-IP selection with core's right-to-left trusted-hop logic. Keep socket peer, forwarded origin and claimed client port as distinct concepts; `EffectiveClientIpResolver` returns an address, not an authenticated forwarded port. Do not pretend delegation alone fixes `getRemotePort()`. Define port behavior explicitly, and test forged leftmost entries, mixed families, malformed chains and untrusted peers in both adapters.
- **Static files (F273):** give resolvers a consistent file identity across case/alias spellings without enabling symlink escape. Verify case-insensitive APFS, case-sensitive paths, index files, root containment and link replacement; obtain Windows evidence for claims involving NTFS aliases. Do not add a general dot-file ban as an incidental fix.
- **HTTP representation/cache details (F275–F278):** reproduce compression/conditional ETag and Vary issues, then fix case-insensitive header handling and cache variation together. Do not remove a legitimate Content-Type from every HEAD or bodyless response indiscriminately.
- Recheck F286 while separating target forms: wire absolute-form authority must not be overridden by an unrelated Host header. Correcting this must retain the proxy-trust boundary for effective origin.

**Validation:** utility and binder regressions plus a small real-socket parser/route test. The physical path must use exact path assertions; `startsWith` cannot catch the lost-segment bug. Keep hostile input sizes small and hard bounded.

### S1d completed — authority, empty names and proxy trust (October 5)

- Absolute HTTP request targets replace the effective Host authority, retain their scheme for request-based origin resolution, and reject malformed authorities with fixed diagnostics. HTTP/1.1 physical Host validation remains before dispatch. Raw paths, query parameters and request copying retain their semantics. This implements reopened F286 ([RFC 9112 §3.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9112.html#section-3.2.2)).
- Explicit empty query/form names are retained; separator-only gaps remain ignored. Lazy lookup, full-map decoding, copying and encoding agree. Default Optional annotation conversion is unchanged.
- Both servlet adapters delegate client address selection to core. Forwarded ports are selected from the same client entry; no port means zero, with socket fallback only when forwarding is unusable/untrusted (C016).
- Allowlisted origin resolution selects the nearest proxy's entry/values without reaching back into client-controlled entries for missing metadata. TRUST_ALL retains its established leftmost behavior. The requirement for trusted edges to sanitize both Forwarded families remains; the review's retracted cross-family client-IP claim is not reopened (F274).
- No public signatures changed. Targeted core checks passed on JDK 17 (123 executed; one SSE test requires JDK 21+) and JDK 25 (124 executed). The JDK 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed. Both servlet adapters passed 13 focused tests against the rebuilt development core JAR. Raw logs and the temporary Maven repository are outside source control.

### S1e/S1f completed — file identity and cache metadata (October 5)

- Independently reproduced the APFS alias defect against the development JAR: `.env` was hidden while `.ENV` was visible. No-follow resolution now obtains actual directory-entry spellings using file identity, without calling symlink-following real-path resolution on request paths. Explicit hard-link names remain separate policy paths; unsupported aliases fail closed. Link/file replacement by application resolvers is checked before response creation. This does not provide an atomic namespace snapshot through later path-backed delivery; docs disclose the stable-directory requirement. NTFS/Windows runtime qualification remains unperformed (F273).
- Default Content-Type is added only for a present body; explicit headers and HEAD representation metadata survive (F276).
- CORS controlled fields update case-insensitively and Vary token merging preserves wildcard and existing dimensions (F277). The List migration already prevented the reported complete Vary loss; the new reproducer confirmed contradictory duplicate Access-Control-Allow-Origin values from the same case-sensitive copy.
- Origin-dependent policies add Vary: Origin to allowed, denied and absent-Origin non-preflight variants, including missing-resource responses. Reject-all does not add it; arbitrary custom policies conservatively vary (F278).
- Compression-enabled 304 responses preserve Accept-Encoding variation without running a planner/codec/body provider (F275). Validator generation remains application-owned: the app must use the same weak semantic validator across identity/compressed variants, or coding-specific strong validators. Automatically weakening every bodyless validator was rejected because it cannot establish what an omitted representation would select. Weak If-Match rejection is required by the protocol, not a validator-comparison bug. User docs explain the supported pattern.
- F260 is closed as the existing documented malformed-header fallback contract; the regression now covers a valid range mixed with an invalid quality. Request Javadocs explicitly describe the combined-input fallback.
- F262 is closed with a documented interpretation limit: [HTML's algorithm](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/form-control-infrastructure.html#multipart/form-data-encoding-algorithm) escapes quotes/CR/LF without escaping literal percent text, so the inverse is ambiguous. Preserve literal quoted parameter spellings rather than silently aliasing distinct user-supplied names. The review itself retracts the filename-star precedence/entity-400 claims; RFC 6266 does not govern multipart form-data, and [RFC 7578 §4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7578.html#section-4.2) prohibits generating filename-star. No speculative multipart parser change was made.
- Four new cache regressions failed before correction. S1's 228 selected tests were exercised across bounded JDK 17/25 runs (one JDK 17 SSE skip; no JDK 25 skips), including the validator ownership clarification. No public signatures changed. Raw evidence stays outside Git.

### S2 — streaming internals

- Replace active `Object.wait` backpressure/start barriers with virtual-thread-friendly waits. Keep interruption, wakeup, queue ownership and notification ordering correct. Do not replace an entire monitor mechanically; preserve atomic state transitions. Cover both HTTP streaming and active MCP output channels.
- Reserve runnable producer capacity **before** committing a response head. On virtual-thread runtimes prefer per-task execution bounded by lifecycle admission; on JDK 17 use bounded platform execution with direct handoff or a bounded precommit wait/rejection. A stream waiting indefinitely behind another long-lived producer must not receive a misleading 200. Reject inline custom-executor execution safely and document the asynchronous executor requirement.
- Make precommit rejection and postcommit failure finish through one exactly-once lifecycle path. Report actual 503/failsafe status, release ownership/reservations, never invoke a rejected producer, and finish OTel spans. Cover streams returned without a Resource Method as well as normal routes.
- Propagate transport handoff failure instead of swallowing it while observers record success. Keep intended status, committed status and stream termination distinct. A producer failure after a valid head was committed still aborts the body; it cannot retroactively become a 500.
- Isolate application cancellation callbacks and termination observers from event loops, timeout threads and unrelated lifecycle-accounting work. Bound retained callback work; do not hide uncooperative residual work or release physical capacity while it still owns resources.
- Separate successful encoder-tail finalization from cancellation cleanup deadlines. A healthy, slow drain should complete; socket progress must prevent a false idle timeout after the writer returns. A genuinely stalled drain remains bounded.
- Avoid treating routine cancellation/interruption as an internal failure; retain meaningful cleanup diagnostics and actual resource-close errors.
- Trampoline synchronous `Flow.Publisher` demand, preserving one-at-a-time flow control without recursive stack growth or leaked lifetime accounting.
- For F068/F195, design a profile-aware response-disconnect policy. Modern MCP SSE closure cancels/fences its request; legacy POST SSE detaches the writer while session cancellation remains explicit. Preserve ordinary HTTP half-close/pipelining. Specify what happens to idle HTTP feeds with no next write; do not claim TCP distinguishes a full peer close from `shutdownOutput()`.

**Validation:** finite loopback tests for capacity rejection, custom executors, slow-but-progressing drain, stalled drain, encoder close, publisher reentrancy and races. Add a JDK 21 subprocess with a small constrained virtual scheduler so carrier exhaustion needs only a handful of sockets. Verify JDK 17/21/25 behavior. OTel rejection/termination tests must prove every span ends exactly once.


### S2a–S2e completed — execution, cleanup and response feedback (October 5)

- **S2a / C003:** HTTP producer start/output waits, MCP outbound capacity waits, and managed resource completion waits park outside their state monitors. Wakeup registration remains atomic with state inspection. A three-case subprocess regression uses two virtual-thread carriers and proves unrelated virtual work progresses. All three cases failed on JDK 21 before the fix and passed afterward; no large client-load probe was run.
- **S2b / F169, F166:** the default HTTP producer executor uses one virtual thread per admitted task on JDK 21+, bounded by lifecycle admission. JDK 17 uses four platform workers per event-loop concurrency unit and direct handoff, with 503 before the stream head on exhaustion. Saturation tests failed before the fix on JDK 17/21 and pass afterward. Arbitrary custom executors must start accepted work promptly; queuing custom executors remain application-owned, and no generic worker-entry deadline is promised. Publisher demand is iterative; a 20,000-item one-at-a-time publisher preserves order without recursive `request()` entry.
- **S2c / F164, F165, F303, F186:** cancelled owned encoders discard tail output during managed cleanup; healthy output waits pause only remaining cleanup grace, while cancellation restores a finite cleanup budget. Actual socket progress renews idle activity after production; a stalled drain still expires. Routine interruption/cancellation wrappers no longer consume the diagnostic slot, and unrelated application/close failures remain visible. Small encoder, injected-clock, drain, interruption and physical-retention regressions reproduced each defect before correction.
- **S2d / F067:** all MCP application cancellation elections reserve the existing bounded callback sidecar before physical handler exit or executor teardown. Handler interruption and response offering/transport detachment precede public hooks. Both ordinary disconnect and deadline tests failed before the fix; they now cover modern, June and November profiles, later deadlines while a hook is blocked, physical-slot retention, and shutdown. Late token registration can still invoke inline on its registering application thread. Existing legacy cancellation and policy retention contracts pass.
- **S2e / F194:** the built-in HTTP transport communicates its actual finite precommit replacement to Soklet's write/finish lifecycle and metrics. Admission and executor rejection each offer one observational stream termination notification, preserving the original stream handle. Source preparation notifications are gated to avoid duplicate delivery. Finite 503 delivery and request finish do not wait for termination observers. A separately bounded allowance on the managed callback executor preserves admitted streams' prepaid jobs; overflow/stopped infrastructure logs omission of the unadmitted-stream notification. Accepted notification work remains physically retained through shutdown. Streams generated without a Resource Method work. Transport handoff failures reach the normal failure observer instead of being swallowed; lazy custom failsafe bodies are not materialized merely for observation.
- Exact core rejection tests reproduced wire 503/observed 200 before the fix. The real OpenTelemetry test uses an unrouted interceptor stream, saturates a custom producer executor, verifies one exported 503 span, and verifies zero active spans and exactly two exported spans after both requests finish. OTel weakly records identity-only completion evidence for finite stream replacements, so a late callback cannot backfill a duplicate span. These markers retain neither request bodies nor spans; collection of the request releases them, and value-equal distinct requests remain separate. A broader regression caught and corrected delayed 503 delivery behind a blocked observer. The admitted/rejected callback concurrency limit is preserved.
- **Documentation/dispositions:** explain head commitment before producer entry (F161), quiesce admission for previously dispatched handlers (C005), cooperative interruption (F157), reverse-proxy buffering configuration (C007), and server-wide streaming timeouts (F162). No new public signatures were introduced. C008/F288/F239/F075/F090 remain separate review checkpoints.

**Development verification:** the final combined selection passed 301 tests on JDK 21 and JDK 25, and 298 executed tests on JDK 17 (three virtual-thread-only skips). It covers streaming lifecycle, cleanup, callbacks, publisher demand, MCP cancellation, finite response feedback, request handling, metrics, compression and cache metadata. The full OTel suite passed 56 tests on JDK 17 against the rebuilt development core JAR in a temporary Maven repository. JDK 21 static analysis passed after the final changes. All builds are serial, with 512 MiB heaps/two processors. Javadoc generation was skipped for the development JAR. Raw logs/artifacts stay in `/private/tmp/soklet-remediation-s2*-20261005/`. These are working-tree checks, not immutable release-candidate evidence.

### S2f completed — response-channel EOF and remote write classification (October 6)

- **F068:** add one internal transport-owner hook, sampled once at dispatch, to close an actively monitored response on input EOF. The built-in MCP owner opts in before response commitment and during committed streams. Normal FIN and deliberate `shutdownOutput()` therefore end the response channel; clients must keep their sending side open while awaiting a response. Precommit/queued and modern work use the existing cancellation boundary. Committed 2025 POST SSE uses the existing writer-detachment boundary: wake blocked reporters, ignore later progress, discard the lost result and retain the handler's physical slot until exit. Session cancellation, deadlines and forced shutdown retain their existing authority; no result is replayed or moved to another connection.
- **F180:** recognized remote-close write exceptions terminate as `CLIENT_DISCONNECTED` without a routine `WRITE_ERROR` transport diagnostic or stack-trace log. Independent write failures retain `WRITE_FAILED` and one write-error observation. Remote-close cause inspection is bounded to 32 links so a source-supplied cyclic exception chain cannot strand the event loop. Existing first-terminal-winner, callback-sidecar, response-offer fencing and physical-retention rules remain intact.
- **F195 disposition — documented transport limit:** ordinary HTTP keeps the default half-close and buffered-pipelining behavior. TCP FIN cannot distinguish a fully closed reader from a peer still reading after closing its output. A quiet ordinary HTTP feed may therefore retain its lifetime until an idle/total timeout or a later write discovers actual connection loss. Disabling both timeouts and making no further writes removes that bound. The public builder Javadoc and core/site streaming guidance state this explicitly and recommend finite timeouts or application heartbeats. A finite test observes ordinary FIN with the response held, proves another connection remains usable and releases the held source at server stop. No public EOF-policy option is added.
- Five new defect regressions fail against saved pre-S2f source: modern FIN cancellation, explicit modern input-end cancellation, pre-offer FIN cancellation on all three revisions, committed legacy FIN detachment and remote write classification. The same six-test selection has one passing independent-write-failure control. The script restores current production source in a `finally` block. Final positive tests additionally cover ordinary idle FIN, default half-close/pipelining and cyclic exception causes. Public progress checks assert one cancellation callback, one stream close, one request finish and physical worker release; legacy checks preserve uncanceled work, inert reporters and capacity through physical exit.
- Local validation: **243 tests across 18 classes pass on JDK 17, 21 and 25**, with zero failures/errors/skips. The serial, offline selection covers transport dispatch/EOF, HTTP streaming lifecycle/publishers/ownership, modern progress, pre-offer races, real 2025 sessions/GET/cancellation, blocked callback isolation and subscriptions. JDK 21 static-analysis clean compilation passes with the same 363 source-warning occurrences / 134 unique normalized path-message entries as S6r, with no additions or removals. Website API/source-link checks pass (**303 owners, 2,215 signatures, 74 checked members, 319 linked types and 18 source links**); protocol-profile evidence checks pass. Finite source derivation remains **27 groups / 286 candidates**. Source-only privacy derivation adds/removes no descriptors in the touched MCP runtime file; the earlier S9 privacy/artifact backlog remains. No supported public Java signature, protocol selector or application timeout changes. Raw logs, source backups and comparisons stay under `/private/tmp/soklet-s2f-*`, outside Git; changes remain unstaged and uncommitted. These are working-tree development checks, not immutable-candidate or external-host qualification.

### S2g completed — HTTP/1.0 streaming rejection feedback (October 6)

- **F185:** the built-in HTTP adapter applies the transport's HTTP/1.0 streaming check before handing off the response, then uses the existing synchronous replacement feedback. `didWriteResponse`, request finish and their metrics callbacks receive the finite 505 response with its replacement headers and empty body. `willWriteResponse` still sees the logical candidate before transport preparation. Replacement feedback also works when the application stream already declares status 505; comparing status alone is insufficient. The event loop shares the same rejection check for direct internal handlers and retains the finite response as its active delivery state. No supported public Java signature changes.
- The original stream handle terminates once with `PROTOCOL_UNSUPPORTED`. Writer entry, input-stream/reader factory acquisition and publisher subscription never start. A blocked termination observer does not delay the finite response, request finish or another buffered request. Buffered HTTP/1.0 delivery, normal HEAD omission and HTTP/1.1 streaming remain supported.
- **F182:** core Javadoc, the README and website streaming guide explain that rejection follows handler/interceptor execution, so their side effects have already occurred. Reverse proxies must use HTTP/1.1 for the upstream connection to Soklet; the website includes explicit nginx protocol and optional buffering configuration linked to nginx's primary documentation. Normal HEAD omission precedes streaming rejection. Simulation has no wire protocol version and does not reproduce this check; a real socket is required.
- Baseline reproduction against saved pre-S2g production source: **three defect regressions fail / three controls pass**, with no errors. The failed cases cover normal 200-to-505 feedback, an original 505 stream and a blocked observer; positive controls cover buffered HTTP/1.0, HEAD and HTTP/1.1 streaming. Current source is restored in a `finally` block. Final tests additionally exercise all four body adapters and direct event-loop rejection without starting its source.
- Local validation: **230 tests across 15 classes pass on JDK 17, 21 and 25**, with zero failures/errors/skips. **57 OpenTelemetry tests pass on JDK 17** against a newly built development core JAR in a separate temporary Maven repository; controlled early/late termination order each exports one 505 span with no active span or shutdown backfill. JDK 21 static-analysis clean compilation passes with the same **363 source-warning occurrences / 134 unique normalized path-message entries** as S2f, with zero additions/removals. Website API/source-link checks and protocol-profile evidence checks pass. Finite source derivation remains **27 groups / 286 candidates**; source-only privacy derivation adds/removes no descriptors in the five touched production files. Earlier S9 privacy/artifact work remains. Serial offline Maven uses two CPUs and 512 MiB; no fuzz/soak is run. Logs, source backups, dependency repository and comparisons remain under `/private/tmp/soklet-s2g-*`, outside Git. New changes are unstaged and uncommitted; pre-existing OTel staging is preserved. These are development checks, not immutable-candidate or external-host qualification.

### S2h completed — cleanup-failure classification and context (October 7)

- **F167:** each admitted HTTP streaming lifetime binds an internal cleanup-diagnostic observer before producer admission. Owned-resource close/abort and publisher cancel failures reach `RESPONSE_STREAM_CLOSE_FAILED` with the original throwable, request, optional resource method and original streaming response. Real HTTP and simulation use the same classification. Framework supervision/deadline failures continue through the coordinator's default diagnostic consumer as `SERVER_INTERNAL_ERROR`; simulator deadline events now use that classification too. The binding is cleared at physical retirement. No supported public Java signature changes.

A finalizer failure may also produce a `PRODUCER_FAILED` termination; an abort or late publisher cancel failure cannot replace an already elected timeout/cancelation. The diagnostic still uses the existing bounded executor, retains its admitted slot until observer exit and remains visible as physical work in incomplete shutdown. Finite response delivery and other buffered requests do not wait for that log observer.

### S2i completed — admitted observer isolation (October 7)

- **F171:** admitted transport termination and diagnostics each use an independent executor with at most lifecycle-capacity workers and a capacity-sized queue. Every reservation still permits one termination notification and one diagnostic. Worker concurrency grows with outstanding execution envelopes, including the short framework return after logical retirement, then shrinks as work exits. Idle workers are reused and expire. This avoids eagerly creating a full capacity-sized pool for sequential traffic, and prevents one blocked cancellation hook, termination observer, cleanup logger or unadmitted rejection observer from consuming another admitted stream's observation worker budget. Application work never runs inline or under the dispatch lock.
- `streamingCallbackConcurrency` continues to bound cancellation batches and unadmitted rejection observers. Their queue reserves one cancellation job per admitted lifetime plus the separate rejection allowance. **The observation concurrency contract changes:** admitted termination and diagnostic workers are each bounded by `streamingLifecycleCapacity`, rather than the callback setting. Observers must remain thread-safe and can be called more concurrently. Builder and lifecycle-observer Javadoc, README, migration guide and website describe the distinction. No supported public Java signature or type is added or changed. Workers are platform threads on all supported JDKs; lifecycle capacity also bounds their maximum count.
- First-terminal-winner, exactly-once task publication, first-diagnostic budget, cleanup deadlines, producer interruption and physical reservation retention remain intact. Snapshot callback counts include both cancellation and termination jobs; deadline dispatcher counts now include both executors, without request/application payloads. Shutdown waits for the new executor as well as cancellation, diagnostics and supervision. Blocking all cancellation workers can still queue later cancellation batches; all queued and running work remains retained. If all lifecycle slots contain blocked observations, new admission still fails and shutdown remains incomplete. Releasing controlled hooks drains every slot and owned worker.
- Five new coordinator regressions fail on pre-S2i production source, with no errors: cancellation-to-termination starvation, termination-to-other-observation starvation, diagnostic starvation, rejection-to-admitted-observation starvation and inability to enter all capacity-reserved observers. They pass after correction. A sixth case exercises 100 lifetimes contending for one admission slot and proves exactly 100 termination notifications and diagnostics, without dropped work at physical retirement/reuse. Four public-runtime tests cover HTTP cancellation/termination/logger stalls, healthy stream admission/retirement, independent subsequent cleanup diagnostics, bounded incomplete shutdown and simulator callers waiting only for their own observers. Existing queue controls now specifically exercise cancellation work; the simulator capacity fixture verifies all three observers can enter despite callback concurrency two, then admission fails while those three remain blocked.
- Local validation: **147 selected cases across 18 classes are green on JDK 17, 21 and 25**, zero failures/errors; JDK 17 executes 146 and skips the existing virtual-thread-only SSE test. **57 OpenTelemetry tests pass on JDK 17** against the rebuilt development core JAR in a separate temporary Maven repository, including early/late rejection observation and exactly-once span retirement. JDK 21 static-analysis clean compilation passes with unchanged **363 source-warning occurrences / 134 unique normalized path-message entries**, zero additions/removals. Website API/source-link checks and protocol-profile evidence checks pass. Finite derivation remains **27 groups / 286 candidates**; source-only privacy comparison adds/removes no descriptors in the three touched source files. ### S2j completed — timeout validation and simulator admission rejection (October 7)

- **F174:** the effective HTTP streaming total and idle timeouts must be nonnegative and representable in nanoseconds. `build()` rejects overflow instead of allowing a request-time `Instant` arithmetic failure or silently saturating an oversized idle timeout. The idle default receives the same check when inherited from `requestBodyTimeout`. Zero still disables a timeout, `null` retains the documented defaults, and one nanosecond through `Duration.ofNanos(Long.MAX_VALUE)` remain accepted. A real HTTP response with both maximum values completes and releases its reservation. No supported public Java signatures change.
- **F254:** HTTP simulation reserves a streaming lifetime during response handoff, before completed write/handling observation. Rejection shares the built-in HTTP failsafe response: finite 503, UTF-8 plain-text content type, `Connection: close` and `HTTP 503: Service Unavailable`. The returned result clears the rejected logical response and preserves its resource method. `didWriteResponse`/`didFinishRequestHandling` observers and metrics receive the finite response exactly once; `willWriteResponse` retains the original pre-admission stream descriptor, matching HTTP. Rejected writers, input streams, readers and publishers are never acquired. A reservation is also retired if handling exits before producer entry.
- Unadmitted simulator rejection notifications use the existing bounded rejection allowance and callback executor, retain the original stream descriptor, and report `BACKPRESSURE` (or `SERVER_STOPPING` when admission has stopped). They do not delay the finite request result. Saturating that allowance skips the extra notification with the existing typed log event and preserves finite 503 delivery; it does not grow callback work. Admitted simulator calls still wait for their own terminal observation. The existing simulator capacity fixture now distinguishes admitted observations from unadmitted rejection notifications. The real HTTP settings fixture now verifies the S2i independent observer bound rather than the former shared pool limit.
- The defect baseline is **three failures / two passing controls**, without errors. Local validation covers **161 selected cases across 20 classes on JDK 17, 21 and 25**, zero failures/errors; JDK 17 skips the existing virtual-thread-only SSE case. The additional rejection-observer capacity control and final cause-identity assertions pass on JDK 17 in the bounded package check. **58 OpenTelemetry tests pass on JDK 17** against the rebuilt development core JAR in an isolated temporary Maven repository; the new simulator admission fixture forces both early and late rejection observation and proves one exported 503 span, no active span leak and no shutdown backfill.
- JDK 21 static-analysis clean compilation passes with unchanged **363 warning occurrences / 134 unique normalized path-message entries**, zero additions/removals. ### S3 — MCP response profiles and deployment

- Normalize **accepted 2025 RPC** success/error responses independently of session configuration. Distinguish those from HTTP framing, authentication, admission, rate-limit and capacity errors; do not indiscriminately turn all 400/404/500 responses into 200.
- Permit externally valid ports for explicitly allowlisted public MCP hostnames behind TLS/proxies. Retain strict authority syntax, one Host occurrence, origin validation, endpoint allowlists and safe automatic loopback defaults. Do not trust arbitrary Forwarded headers to authorize a hostname.
- Check initialization header/body rules, missing-version requests with a verified session, unknown notifications and terminated-session responses against each exact revision. Unsupported 2025-03-26 remains unsupported; do not introduce an implicit “latest.”
- Fix safe legacy projections, such as removing optional June-incompatible resource-link icons. Treat non-object structured results/output schemas separately: document the supported shape or supply a defined legacy representation where valid. Do not silently wrap values, drop required schema data or execute a tool twice.
- Align annotation-time validation with builder/runtime validation. Configuration errors should name the endpoint, operation and offending revision.
- Check rejected legacy GET simulation and response headers while changing error projection.
- Update the quickstart to show the explicit protocol selections needed for common 2025 clients, alongside modern usage. Keep dated, tested host claims separate from wire capability claims.
- **C002:** keep the stateless limitation explicit. Session-disabled 2025 progress requests currently lack cross-request cancellation. Recommend sessions when cancellation is required. Adding a second stateless request-ID ownership registry is a separate design, not a blanket disconnect-to-cancel fix.

**Validation:** one finite table across both 2025 revisions, session on/off, finite JSON/POST SSE, admission denied, invalid arguments, unknown resource/cursor, handler failure and missing/incorrect headers. Replay one pinned released SDK or host client before making a client-interoperability claim.

### S3c completed — negotiation, client notifications and configuration validation (October 5)

Invalid, unsupported and duplicate selectors still fail closed. This intentionally narrows the review's suggested unconditional header override: a June header does not override a November body when both revisions are configured. Modern metadata/mirrors cannot downgrade into legacy framing. The documentation states this limit; no implicit latest or March fallback was added.
- **F026:** every well-formed 2025 notification passes fresh admission/request limiting and, when enabled, verified session acquisition. Unsupported notifications such as roots/list_changed are ignored with empty 202; malformed metadata/parameters still fail, admission/rate denials remain enforced, and retired/wrong-owner IDs receive neutral 404. Only initialized/cancelled apply their existing effects. Modern unsupported notifications retain 400. No roots/sampling/server-initiated request capability is advertised.

Endpoint version/subset and tool/prompt/resource/Skill errors identify configured names, path and revisions; normalized subscription-view failures include the exact revision lacking the resource surface.

Successful/closed SSE semantics and capture bounds retain regression coverage. Immutable response headers now support case-insensitive lookups. Core and website compatibility pages describe negotiation and ignored notifications.

### S4 — legacy session liveness and control traffic

- Split client activity/quiescence from background authorization-maintenance retention. Idle clients must logically expire despite grant renewal; ongoing physical callbacks must still be fenced, cancelled and accounted until they really exit.
- Separate persistent session evidence from transient request/body retention. A large accepted POST must not permanently consume the session's ability to unsubscribe, ping, cancel or delete.
- Give verified cancellation/termination control traffic a bounded path independent of ordinary request quota exhaustion. Keep framing, identity, owner, session and request-ID checks. Exempting every unauthenticated notification would create a new abuse path.
- Review per-owner/global/control budgets together. Fix avoidable starvation and misleading retry hints; do not solve it with unbounded memory, unlimited owner partitions or larger queues alone. If global fairness remains a supported limitation, document a concrete deployment configuration and the bounds.
- Classify explicit authorization denial separately from transient authorizer failure. Bounded retry must never extend an expired lease or deliver beyond a revocation fence. If an established URI grant cannot recover, provide a recoverable protocol outcome, such as retiring the session so the client reinitializes, rather than silently forgetting a subscription forever.
- Reopen Appendix A F027: distinguish a fresh invalidation, a failed offer retry and an already-delivered notification. Test two concurrently connected GET streams and reconnection after successful delivery against the 2025 single-stream rule. Preserve bounded coalescing; do not introduce replay/history.

**Validation:** injected monotonic clock for expiry/renewal; small owner limits; a large-body test under a strict heap cap; cancellation while quota is exhausted; DELETE during an active POST; stale authorization completion and concurrent GET handoff. No wall-clock 24-hour runs.

### S4a completed — client liveness versus renewal ownership (October 5)

- **F024:** private session accounting now distinguishes physical client work (initialization, POST and GET) from grant authorization ownership. Only completion of client work updates client quiescence. Background renewal retains physical references/evidence but no longer refreshes client idle time or prevents logical expiry/same-owner idle eviction. Logical retirement still fences grants immediately, and stale authorization cannot restore a retired session. Existing actual client-work and absolute-lifetime semantics remain in force; default durations and public signatures are unchanged.
- Injected-clock regressions reproduced renewed grants keeping abandoned sessions alive at the idle deadline. Both exact legacy revisions now cover repeated completed renewals, an unfinished renewal at expiry, neutral not-found lookup, stale renewal rejection, idempotent physical release and final zero accounting. Four unfinished callbacks also cover same-owner eviction: a new initialization can replace the oldest idle logical session while all old callback evidence remains physically retained until exit. No live waiting or sustained fuzz run was used.
- **Still pending after S4a:** request-evidence separation and control traffic. S4b below completes the persistent/transient byte separation; the remaining S4 work is listed there.

### S4b completed — persistent versus transient request evidence (October 5)

- **F019:** session initialization/POST evidence has separate, finite session/owner/global budgets from persistent metadata, GETs and historical URI grants. Production transient limits derive from configured body/header ceilings: with `R = maximumBodyBytes + maximumHeaderBytes + 16,384`, the budgets are `2R`, `4R` and `32R`. A schema-valid large request no longer deterministically fails because its body exceeds the 1 MiB persistent session floor. Transient memory pressure has its own internal result and maps to HTTP 503, recovering on actual request exit.
- **F020 (persistent-byte deadlock):** a full persistent grant budget no longer prevents a transient unsubscribe, ping, notification or owner-verified DELETE. Grant establishment atomically promotes the complete shared historical subscribe evidence into the persistent budget only after capacity checks; no body/credentials are dropped, no duplicate raw copy is added, and a failed promotion changes no reservation or grant. Long-lived grant budgets themselves remain finite.
- Logical cancellation/retirement does not release unfinished requests or callbacks. Diagnostic retained bytes continue to include both budgets. Tests preserve same-owner eviction, cross-owner protection, stale authorization fencing, repeated physical completion and final zero accounting. Pressure fixtures now fill actual persistent metadata or the separate transient pool rather than assuming an in-flight body occupies persistent metadata space.
- New regressions cover a 1.3 MiB tool call with and without sessions on both legacy revisions; explicit transient boundaries with ignored physical work; 63 retained grants filling the persistent budget while unsubscribe and session deletion succeed; rejected promotion rollback; and the retired callback's complete evidence remaining charged until exit. All workstation builds use a 512 MiB heap and two active processors; raw output stays under `/private/tmp/soklet-remediation-s3e-s4b-20261005/`.
- **Validation:** the selected 363-test API/session/wire set completed on JDK 17 with its sole initial failure being the newly approved factory's nullability fingerprint; its 38-test reflection class then passed after verifying and updating only that three-record delta. The same 363-test set passed on JDK 25 with zero skips. JDK 21 static analysis passed. Offline website API/linked-source checks passed, and the new core/website resource-error snippets compiled on JDK 17. These are development checks, not API-ledger refreeze or immutable-candidate qualification.

The complete S4 slice and S5–S10 remain unfinished.

### S4c completed — independently bounded verified control traffic (October 5)

- **F020 (ordinary quota/queue/transient saturation):** small, valid owner-verified 2025 session ping, unsubscribe, initialized and cancellation messages use a separate finite control reservation and bypass ordinary request limiting. Fresh admission, authentication, owner/path/revision/generation binding, correlation-ID bounds and active-ID collision checks remain. Malformed/oversized controls, unsupported notifications and session-disabled traffic retain ordinary limiting. The control body ceiling is 16 KiB; evidence per control is capped at configured header bytes plus 32,768 bytes.
- Separate physical bounds are four controls per session, eight per owner and 64 globally. Ordinary in-flight bytes/32 request-ID registrations cannot consume those reserved slots. Cleanup also has four simultaneous reservations and 64 attempts per sliding second per effective authorization partition, with at most 256 retained partitions. GET/URI maintenance uses a separate demand budget. Full control capacity returns HTTP 503. Logical completion/retirement does not free physical evidence or control slots early.
- DELETE transport admission now shares the independent bounded session-owner dispatcher rather than ordinary tool execution. The controller's cancellation deadline and actual callback-exit ownership remain enforced; HTTP admission can still deny termination. DELETE evidence remains charged until the request physically completes. No public signature/type was added or changed; existing limiter and authorizer Javadocs and core/website guidance describe the behavior change.
- Regressions cover full ordinary byte and 32-ID pools, correlation collisions/wrong owners, four/session/eight/owner/64/global control saturation, logical-versus-physical release, and final zero accounting. Real HTTP tests fill a one-handler/one-queued-request server while ordinary quota denies controls, then verify ping/ack/cancellation and DELETE still work. Admission denial, invalid/oversized cancellation, integer exponent bounds and neutral wrong-owner/session-not-found behavior remain enforced. The GET maintenance pressure fixture now fills real tool handlers rather than depending on DELETE occupying ordinary execution.

### S4d completed — recoverable established URI grant loss (October 5)

- **F025:** transient URI renewal exceptions/timeouts fence delivery and permit at most three consecutive failed attempts under the existing lease, with 50 ms then 100 ms retry delays. Success resets the failure count. Capacity retries keep their existing bounded scheduler/demand policy; neither retry path extends an expired lease. A callback that ignores cancellation retains physical ownership and the per-URI authorization slot until actual exit, preventing overlap.
- Explicit renewal denial, exhausted failed attempts, lease expiry or total grant expiry on an established URI grant now retire the whole legacy session. GETs close, current calls are cancelled and subsequent freshly verified lookup returns neutral HTTP 404, allowing reinitialization/resubscription. No unsupported subscription-ended notification is invented. Initial subscribe rejection and explicit unsubscribe do not themselves retire a session; generation checks prevent a stale denial/failure from retiring newer permission. Original subscribe credentials and the latest successful application context remain the renewal evidence.
- Injected-clock tests cover both exact revisions and all four unrecoverable grant-loss causes, session/GET fencing, unfinished renewal evidence, rejected stale renewal and final zero accounting. Public simulations cover transient recovery with dirty URI hints held behind the fence, exactly three failed attempts followed by successful new initialization, ignored timeout with lease expiry/no callback overlap, detached-grant denial, and refreshed Bearer credentials requiring reinitialization after historical-grant revocation. Real named-host recovery remains pending qualification.
- **Development validation:** the selected 371-test legacy/wire/API suite passed on JDK 17 and JDK 25 with zero failures, errors or skips. JDK 21 static analysis passed with no new warnings compared with S4b's final run; a final 42-test public control/recovery recheck then passed on JDK 17 against that compiled source. Offline website API/reference and core-link checks passed. Workstation builds remain capped at 512 MiB and two active processors; raw output stays under `/private/tmp/soklet-s4-control-recovery-*.log`, outside source control. This does not constitute immutable-candidate qualification or API-ledger refreeze.
- **At completion of S4d:** owner/global fairness disposition and retry hints (F018), single-stream notification delivery (F027), and store-maintenance scaling (F021) remained. S4e/S4f below complete those items; S5–S10 remain unfinished.

### S4e completed — one stream per legacy invalidation (October 5)

- **F027:** a store reservation now claims the session/family-or-URI key atomically before offering it to a channel. Concurrent flush snapshots cannot retain that key on two GETs. The channel reports a fully written HTTP chunk separately from dropped/failed/partially written frames, with both callbacks outside its lock and release in a `finally` path. Simulation treats accepted capture as complete delivery.
- A fully written catalog hint remains suppressed until a freshly admitted corresponding list rearms it. A fully written URI hint clears only its own current dirty generation. Opening another GET or reconnecting does not resend a completed notification. A new publisher change during a queued frame remains pending for a subsequent hint.
- Failed offers and dropped unwritten frames preserve pending state. Fencing or retiring a GET does not release its notification key before the writer reports write/drop: the final socket write may already have won. This closes the write-completion-versus-fence race without a store lock at the socket boundary. Late cleanup retains the exact notification accounting and cannot clear another attempt.
- Regressions cover complete chunk versus partial/drop disposition, competing flush snapshots, complete write before offer acknowledgment, fence before deferred completion, failed offer, unwritten disconnect, queued newer invalidations, two simultaneous real GETs, and reconnect on both exact 2025 revisions. Core and website documentation now distinguish pending delivery from replay and link the exact single-stream requirement.

### S4f completed — capacity disposition and bounded session maintenance (October 5)

- **F018 disposition:** retain the documented owner-isolation policy. No cross-owner eviction or silent stateless downgrade is introduced. Removed the invented `Retry-After: 1` from session count/evidence/control capacity responses: expiry, DELETE, or physical callback exit determine recovery. Application rate-limit decisions retain their explicit retry hints.
- Core reference, website and `McpSessionConfig` Javadoc now state that 16 owners at the default 16-session cap can fill 256 global slots and deny new owners for the abandoned clients' 24-hour idle window. The separate anonymous ceiling remains 64. A concrete four-per-owner/1,024-global/15-minute-idle example explains both its owner population and remaining global/byte limits. An injected-clock default-count regression verifies denial at 23 hours and reclamation at 24 hours without a workstation soak.
- **F021:** existing-session request, GET and grant operations check expiry only for their referenced bounded session. The periodic maintenance sweep and new-session allocation still inspect global expiry. Indexed endpoint-path/revision membership limits publisher/grant/delivery scans to their relevant sessions; retirement removes that membership. Publisher URIs are validated and parsed once per event rather than once per session. No new application-facing configuration or diagnostic signatures are added. The global store lock, allocation/reclamation scans and periodic sweeps remain; this is not a lock-free or arbitrary-scale claim.
- Touched-session expiry rejects expired IDs immediately with neutral 404; unrelated expiry remains the periodic sweep's responsibility. Tests verify both paths, physical retention after logical retirement, default count saturation/recovery, and capacity responses without an invented retry time. Existing authorization, grant, control-path and actual transport tests remain required.

Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed with no new warnings compared with S4d. The capacity-configuration example compiled against the current classes; offline website API/reference and 18 core-file link checks passed. Both repository whitespace checks passed and both indexes remain empty. Raw logs remain under `/private/tmp/soklet-s4-complete-*.log`, outside Git. No immutable-candidate, API-ledger-refreeze or named-host qualification is claimed.

**Next:** S5 publisher/subscription scheduling, policy reconciliation and quiescence. S5–S10 and remaining S2 work are not complete.

### S5 — subscription scheduling and authorization

- Replace “submit one job per listener, terminate on scheduler queue rejection” with fair, bounded dirty-work scheduling/coalescing tied to admitted subscriptions. Keep an aggregate memory bound and renewal deadlines; an expired authorization cannot remain deliverable while waiting for a retry. Separate genuine slow-client pressure from a shared scheduler's transient saturation.
- Restrict observer/metrics deferral to the owning operation. No server-global deferral may span a blocking client write; bound pending metric records and drain them away from request processors and shutdown-critical locks.
- Fix A→B→A catalog digest/coalescing behavior. The baseline must represent what the current writer is guaranteed to observe, rather than simply treating any coalesced offer as delivered. Test both accepted and pending/coalesced frames.
- Retain resource/catalog dirtiness through reconcile and authorization fences so there is a bounded catch-up after a successful reauthorization. Never deliver queued events against stale identities or generations.
- Fence listen admission and legacy GET establishment against quiesce. Every pending operation must receive a response/termination outcome and release its drain claim; no new long-lived stream opens after quiescence.
- Close the task activation race with a bounded activation-time projection or equivalent generation fence. Enforce current catalog-access policy and capability checks on **notification** task results, using the same policy protection as polling; task ownership alone is insufficient.
- Verify terminal subscription responses and denial/failure reason classification against the modern specification.

**Validation:** deterministic barrier tests for admission/quiesce and reconciliation, task completion between lookup and activation, hidden/revoked task results, catalog A→B→A, and burst fan-out with reduced worker capacity. Use one bounded larger fan-out check in CI after small tests pass; avoid repeating workstation load tests.

#### S5a — metric delivery isolation and bounds

- **F038:** subscription-maintenance recording signals the existing dedicated metric-drain scheduler. The default observer atomically marks those records for asynchronous delivery when enqueuing them: merely scheduling a worker was insufficient, because a concurrent ordinary request drain could adopt the record and block a protocol worker on its collector. The metric executor has one worker and one queue slot, with coalesced generation signals.
- **F039:** progress reporting holds only its invocation's progress lock while enqueueing the update. It records and drains `ProgressEmitted` after that lock is released, without a server-global deferral across the potentially blocking write. Repeated accepted progress remains suppressed; decreasing progress remains rejected; failed/interrupted writes do not record accepted progress.
- Opening metric deferrals and asynchronous drain attempts no longer wait for an existing callback or deferral. A blocked asynchronous drain leaves a delivery requirement, which is signaled again when the active delivery or the final deferral ends. Serialized delivery and retained-record FIFO order remain intact; cross-thread lifecycle deferral remains supported.
- Pending semantic records are bounded to 4,096 per built-in server. New ordinary records are omitted at the bound; `ServerStarted`/`ServerStopped` reclaim an ordinary record. Discarding an omitted/evicted provisional record is harmless to its wire result. No request/context/throwable carrier is retained in a queued metric entry. API/Javadoc and website prose explain the overflow behavior and that counters/gauges may be incomplete afterward. No new application-facing type or signature is added.
- Small barrier tests cover a pending progress write, collector/deferral contention, bounded pending records, lifecycle priority and wake-up races. A real two-subscription test stalls maintenance collection and verifies `Soklet.close()` reaches `GRACEFUL_TERMINATION` before releasing the collector. Raw logs remain under `/private/tmp/soklet-s5a-*.log`.
- **Still pending:** F045 fan-out scheduling, operation-scoped request-transition deferral, catalog A→B→A, dirtiness through authorization/reconciliation fences, admission/quiesce races, task activation/access checks and the remaining S5 verification items. Short runtime-transition and cross-thread lifecycle deferrals still use the shared metric gate. This slice does not claim complete S5 or release readiness.

**S5a validation:** 242 selected metrics, subscription, progress, task, lifecycle, legacy-transport and public API reflection tests passed on each of Corretto 17 and 25, with zero failures, errors or skips. This includes the real two-subscription blocked-collector shutdown regression. Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed; comparing warning path/text without line numbers against S4f found 376 existing warnings and zero new warnings. Offline website API/reference checks passed (303 owners, 2,215 signatures, 74 members and 319 linked types); all 18 core-file links passed. Both repository whitespace checks passed and indexes remain empty. Logs remain under `/private/tmp/soklet-s5a-*.log`, outside Git. This is local development evidence, not immutable-candidate validation or API-ledger refreeze.

#### S5b — admitted maintenance slots and resource catch-up

- **F045:** replace the 128-job global overflow queue with identity-owned coalesced slots reserved during modern listen admission. Each pending/active subscription reserves four independent owners (authorization, catalog, tasks, resources); the aggregate admission cap follows the connection cap, with a finite 8,192-subscription fallback when transport connections are unlimited. Existing partition quotas remain shared with legacy GETs. Admission rejects new listens before opening streams; fan-out cannot evict already admitted peers. An intrusive FIFO permits constant-time owner removal without retained tombstones. Each worker executes one job, returns to the protocol executor tail, and puts an owner's continuation behind waiting peers; an owner cannot run on two workers concurrently.
- Legacy maintenance keeps its existing four-job/rate/physical-callback quotas and uses a fresh temporary scheduler identity for each dispatch. Retirement, executor retry, shutdown, and an old worker completing after owner-identity reuse have bounded cleanup coverage.
- Authorization checks and catalog callback deadlines are reserved at dispatch. Queue waits do not consume callback timeout, extend old leases, or extend total stream lifetime. Queued reconciliation captures the latest fenced generation and context at dispatch; an expired owner closes without entering its queued authorizer.
- **F047:** accepted resource-list, URI, and localization invalidations retain bounded dirty state through reconciliation (one list marker plus at most 256 selected URI identities). A successful fresh grant schedules catch-up; denial/expiry/termination discard it. Resource hints remain dirty until a full frame write, and keyed release preserves a newer hint racing that write. Frames read an immutable volatile authority snapshot containing the current authorization generation and lease at the socket boundary. The guard never acquires the subscription owner lock beneath the channel lock; fencing and retirement clear the snapshot. A bounded cross-thread regression holds the owner lock while another thread verifies the guard can return. Ordinary renewal does not invalidate a still-authorized partially written frame merely because it replaces the registration object; reconciliation removes revoked unwritten frames and aborts a revoked partial frame.
- Small deterministic tests cover eight-owner fan-out (the original implementation rejected six), duplicate coalescing, fairness, owner serialization, reservation bounds/reclamation, shutdown continuations, temporary dispatches, executor saturation/retry, six seconds of simulated queue wait, queued lease expiry, fenced URI/list/localization catch-up, denial, and registration replacement during a resource offer. The existing reconciliation test now requires a retained hint to catch up only after the fresh grant; its old expectation discarded the hint.
- Application-facing signatures are unchanged. Core and website guidance now describe the actual admission/memory bounds and distinguish catch-up hints from durable replay or client receipts. Raw validation logs stay under `/private/tmp`.

**S5b validation:** final affected-regression runs passed all 357 selected tests on each of Corretto 17 and 25, with zero failures, errors or skips. Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed with the same 376 warnings as S5a and no new warnings. Offline website API and 18 core-file link checks passed. Both repository whitespace checks passed; both indexes remain empty. The new authority snapshot and its owner-lock independence were included in the final runs. These are working-tree development checks; no API refreeze, named-host qualification, immutable-candidate or release acceptance gate is claimed.

**Validation note:** the existing `liveDuplicateTemplateUnsubscribeAndDisconnectedGapPreserveOnlyCurrentUriGrant` regression timed out during three earlier combined JDK 17 runs, while isolated checks and the subsequent combined runs passed. The timeout was at the disconnected-gap resource catch-up read; it is not claimed as a diagnosed or separately fixed legacy bug. Keep that observed timing sensitivity in subsequent S5 validation. Temporary diagnostic edits were removed; no raw run data is added to Git.

**Remaining S5:** catalog A→B→A/write baselines (F046), quiesce/admission races (F052/F053), task activation and current policy checks (F083/F084), the listed terminal/localization verifications, and operation-scoped short-transition metric deferral. S5 and the release are not complete.

#### S5c — catalog observations covered by pending hints

- **F046:** advance the modern tools/prompts comparison digest for both accepted and coalesced catalog hints. Completing that projection clears its dirty generation only when no newer invalidation arrived. The baseline records an observation covered by a future hint; it is not a delivery receipt or retained catalog snapshot.
- A socket can expose the entire SSE event payload before the final HTTP chunk delimiter is written. Merely keeping a coalescing key until full chunk completion is therefore insufficient. Catalog `offerCoalescingIf` now coalesces only into a wholly unwritten frame. Once writing starts, a newer observation reserves a successor within the existing frame/byte capacity; later observations coalesce into that successor until it starts. The channel indexes the newest keyed chunk and removes that entry only when releasing that same chunk, so completing the predecessor cannot erase its successor's key. Ordinary and authorization-guarded resource coalescing retain their prior semantics.
- Both tool and prompt regressions failed at the missing back-and-forth notification before the baseline fix. They now drive two opposite oscillations with pending hints, consume the hint, re-list through the public simulator to verify the actual catalog, require the next change, and reject unchanged duplicates. Two transport regressions separately reproduced the started-frame gap and verify successor-key retention and capacity at the payload/chunk-delimiter boundary.
- Application-facing signatures are unchanged. Core/website guidance describes the corrected invalidation baseline and the partial-write boundary. A stale adjacent claim that tool/prompt list-change filters are never acknowledged was corrected to match configured caller-visible projections.
- **Distributed deployment review:** existing task storage, event publishers, admission/authorization and limiter hooks may use application-owned distributed services. Events and policy/localization invalidation must reach every eligible server. Explicit 2025 sessions remain framework-owned and node-local, including client metadata, cancellation correlations and resource grants; no shared session-store SPI exists. Session-enabled traffic requires affinity to the initializing node and reinitialization/resubscription after loss. Application storage does not remove that restriction. Modern/session-disabled routing and portable application/framework cursors have their documented data/key/catalog requirements. Server capacity bounds remain local. Javadoc and website deployment guidance make these limits explicit; this slice does not add a distributed session backend or claim transparent failover. Portable legacy sessions with arbitrary node routing remain an unsupported capability; implementing it requires a separately reviewed storage/coordination SPI, beyond the existing application callbacks.

**S5c validation:** 397 selected subscription, legacy transport, channel/stream, scheduler, metrics, lifecycle, cancellation and public API reflection tests passed on each of Corretto 17 and 25, with zero failures, errors or skips. The two catalog oscillation tests and two started-frame transport tests each reproduced their failures before their respective fixes. Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed with the same 376 warnings as S5b and no new warnings. Offline website API/reference and all 18 core-file link checks passed. Both repository whitespace checks passed and both indexes remain empty. The earlier S5b legacy reconnect timing note remains tracked; its passing result here does not diagnose that earlier failure. These are working-tree development checks, not an API refreeze, named-host or immutable-candidate release gate. Raw logs remain under `/private/tmp/soklet-s5c-*.log`, outside Git.

**Remaining S5 after S5c:** quiesce/admission races (F052/F053), task activation and current policy checks (F083/F084), the listed terminal/localization verifications, and operation-scoped short-transition metric deferral. S5 and the release are not complete.

#### S5d — subscription admission during graceful shutdown

- **F052:** a validated modern listen that reaches its capacity or final activation check after quiesce now returns a finite HTTP `503` JSON-RPC error with its request ID and `Connection: close`. For an existing pending reservation, shutdown elects and offers that response before canceling the bounded policy checks and their protocol waiter. The normal finite-response body listener retains the transport lifecycle lease through delivery, and physical policy/executor work keeps its existing accounting. Concurrent completion cannot take the response callback twice. Established streams retain their graceful terminal path.
- **F053:** modern and legacy stream activation share the subscription admission gate. A legacy GET paused before attachment, inside HTTP admission or owner resolution, or in the protocol queue cannot establish a new stream after quiesce. Pending GET shutdown returns a bodyless HTTP `503` with `Connection: close`; HTTP DELETE cleanup and ordinary finite requests retain their existing graceful-drain behavior. The legacy GET's final head-ownership election is serialized with gate closure while stream installation remains protected by its owner lock. Session-store callbacks and response delivery occur outside the admission gate.
- The new deterministic real-socket regressions pause modern CORS, admission and subscription authorization; pause legacy CORS, HTTP admission and owner resolution for both supported 2025 revisions; and queue a GET behind an admitted finite request. They verify finite responses plus the public `GRACEFUL` aggregate and MCP component result while the client socket remains open. Separate coverage verifies clean completion of established modern/legacy streams and that a blocked protocol-error metrics collector cannot delay the pending-listen response or shutdown. The original implementation dropped all three modern response heads and opened the CORS-paused GET after quiesce. The queue test controls the internal protocol executor, distinct from application handler capacity.
- Application-facing signatures and the stable `McpSessionConfig` naming remain unchanged. Core and website shutdown guidance describe the finite precommit responses. The distributed session-store design remains a proposal; this slice does not implement it. Metrics for the shutdown response drain asynchronously, preserving S5a's shutdown boundary.
