Corretto 25 passed the final 359-test selection with zero failures, errors or skips. Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed; comparison against S5c by warning path/text without line numbers found the same 376 existing warnings and zero new warnings. Offline website API/reference verification passed (303 owners, 2,215 signatures, 74 members and 319 linked types), and all 18 core-file links passed. Both repository whitespace checks passed and indexes remain empty. Logs are under `/private/tmp/soklet-s5d-*.log`, outside Git. This is development validation, not immutable-candidate release validation or API-ledger refreeze.

**Remaining S5 after S5d:** task activation and current policy checks (F083/F084), the listed terminal/localization verifications, and operation-scoped short-transition metric deferral. S5 and the release are not complete.

#### S5e — task activation and notification policy

- **F083:** pending capacity reservations retain task-change generations within the validated, at-most-256-ID request filter. Activation drops IDs absent from the final acknowledgment and elects one fair task-projection job after the response handoff. The acknowledgment remains first. This is an event-generation fence: it preserves the existing absence of unconditional initial snapshots and pre-listen replay. Events that arrive during authorization now trigger current authoritative projection after activation, including a fast completion and a transition to an input request the client cannot support.
- **F084:** task notifications open a fresh catalog-access session with the subscription's current authorized context and pass it to the same detailed task-snapshot adapter used by polling. Hiding the originating tool withholds its result and skips sanitization. S5i below replaces the interim status-only completed projection with suppression of that notification and a fixed error on polling. Current task input-capability checks remain enforced. Manager lookup, policy and sanitization share one bounded application invocation using `subscriptionAuthorizationTimeout`, capped by the remaining lease and stream lifetime. Application failure or timeout suppresses that generation; a later event or reauthorization may retry. A callback that ignores cancellation retains its owner's task-projection slot until physical exit, with coalesced follow-up work returning to the fair scheduler's tail.
- Task frames use the existing lock-free stream/generation/lease write authority. The absolute projection deadline is checked again at channel admission after encoding. A later authorization fence discards an unwritten frame or fails a partially written frame; notification state records a terminal as delivered only after the complete chunk is written. A suppressed unwritten terminal can therefore be projected under a replacement grant. Distinct frames keep independent bounded transport reservations, preserving status ordering without merging different task results. Write guards do not acquire the request-control lock under the channel lock.
- Six new real-listener regressions cover activation-time completion and private-ID filtering, current input capabilities, hidden results matching polling, timeout/physical callback retention with peer progress, replacement authorization context at the offer boundary, and a late offer crossing its projection deadline. Two scheduler regressions cover accepted-ID filtering and the absence of an invented initial snapshot. The first three tests reproduced the reported failures before the fixes. Public Java signatures are unchanged; core, builder Javadoc and website guidance describe the corrected behavior and existing timeout setting.

**S5e validation:** the final 46 task-subscription/scheduler tests passed on each of Corretto 17 and 25. The broader Corretto 17 selection passed 388 subscription, task, metrics, lifecycle, cancellation, legacy transport/simulator and public API reflection tests; the final task selection then included the additional late-offer regression (389 distinct tests covered). The corresponding 388-test Corretto 25 run had one error: `liveDuplicateTemplateUnsubscribeAndDisconnectedGapPreserveOnlyCurrentUriGrant` timed out at the tools-list notification after unsubscribe, after its disconnected-gap resource update had already arrived. Earlier S5b failures were at the disconnected-gap resource update. A subsequent 68-test Corretto 25 selection passed the complete 46-test task selection and all 22 legacy subscription tests. This retry does not diagnose or fix the intermittent reconnect failure. Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed with the same 376 warnings as S5d and zero new warnings after comparison by path/text without line numbers. Offline website API/reference checks passed (303 owners, 2,215 signatures, 74 members and 319 linked types), as did all 18 core-file links. Both repository whitespace checks passed and both indexes remain empty. Raw logs are under `/private/tmp/soklet-s5e-*.log`, outside Git. #### S5f — duplicate legacy notification flushes

- Diagnosed the intermittent legacy reconnect/subsequent catalog timeouts. Event publishing, GET activation and timer retries can select the same dirty notification before either reserves its bytes. The winning flush claims the key; the losing flush previously interpreted an empty reservation as capacity pressure and closed a healthy GET with `BACKPRESSURE`. Temporary lifecycle diagnostics captured this erroneous close approximately one millisecond after reconnect. Both the resource catch-up and later tools-list read reproduced in bounded repetitions.
- Byte reservation now returns its outcome atomically under the session-store lock: accepted, stale/already claimed, owner capacity or global capacity. Only actual capacity rejection may shed a writer. Pressure scope comes from that same reservation outcome; a later accounting read cannot change it. Existing per-owner/global ceilings, guarded-write authorization, release accounting and channel-full handling remain in force. Application-facing signatures are unchanged.
- Two deterministic socket regressions force a competing flush between attempt selection and reservation while retaining the winning frame unwritten. Both failed before the fix by observing that the healthy GET was closed. They require delivery exactly once, continued use of the same GET for another notification, and cover both supported 2025 revisions and both resource/catalog keys. Store checks separately distinguish duplicate claims from unclaimed keys hitting the exact owner/global limits.

**S5f validation:** both deterministic regressions failed before the fix on Corretto 25; after the fix, those two regressions and all 30 legacy grant-store tests passed. The original reconnect/unsubscribe scenario passed 30 serial repetitions on Corretto 25, with each repetition exercising both legacy revisions. After removing the temporary repeat annotation, the related 391-test subscription/task/metrics/lifecycle/legacy/API reflection selection passed separately on Corretto 17 and 25 with zero failures or errors. Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed with exactly the same 376 warning path/text entries as S5e and zero new warnings. Both repository whitespace checks passed; both indexes remain empty. Temporary state/lifecycle logging was removed; the private, instance-local reservation test seam remains for the deterministic regressions. Raw diagnosis/build output stays under `/private/tmp/soklet-s5f-*.log`. These are working-tree development checks; candidate release gates and API-ledger refreeze remain separate.

**Remaining S5 after S5f:** the listed terminal/localization verifications and operation-scoped short-transition metric deferral. S5 and the release are not complete.

#### S5g — catalog opening and localization fallback

- **F117:** tool/prompt subscription digests now accept canonical text produced by the default `USE_DEFAULT_TEXT` localization policy after provider or lookup failure. Initial baselines and later projections follow the same policy as ordinary catalog list responses. Recovery to translated text, or a later return to canonical text, can emit a list-change hint; an unchanged canonical view emits none. `FAIL_REQUEST` still rejects an initial failure with the sanitized internal error and releases reserved subscription capacity. Existing projection deadlines, cancellation and output bounds remain in force.
- **F048:** an ordinary catalog invalidation during opening no longer restarts subscription authorization. The activation transition installs the already-computed baseline under the current grant and reserves bounded catalog catch-up behind the acknowledgment. Actual authorization-generation or grant changes still require a fresh authorization. A concurrent localization invalidation discards stale pre-rendered terminal text while preserving the selected stream audience and response headers.
- Rendering fallback does not replace the localization context required by an explicitly configured catalog access policy. Context creation failure still rejects the request before policy evaluation, and a failing policy never becomes an allow decision. Application-facing signatures are unchanged. Core and website guidance explain this boundary and opening catch-up.
- Seven new public-runtime regressions cover provider/lookup fallback and recovery, sanitized strict-policy rejection with capacity reclamation, explicit policy context failure, catalog churn without repeated authorization, changed baselines with acknowledgment-first catch-up, and simultaneous localization/catalog invalidation. Five reproduced their failures against the prior runtime; strict-policy and required-context safeguards already passed. The existing terminal-pre-render regression now expects canonical text after invalidation rather than an opening retry; it still rejects both stale and unnecessarily refreshed terminal snapshots. The over-limit language-preference fixture now uses internal whitespace that survives the earlier HTTP field normalization, preserving its actual 4,097-code-unit input.

**Remaining S5 after S5g:** modern terminal-response/reason classification (F050), the remaining task-result disposition review (F085), and operation-scoped short-transition metric deferral. S5 and the release are not complete.

#### S5h — server-initiated modern subscription closure

- **F050:** verified the exact modern [subscription closure rule](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/patterns/subscriptions#graceful-closure). It recommends a successful completion response when the server ends the listen itself, rather than the error response suggested in the external review. Established modern listens ended by denial, lease expiry, required-check failure or bounded queue pressure now fence delivery and cancel maintenance, then use the existing separate bounded terminal reservation to send the tagged empty `complete` result when the transport remains usable. Existing maximum-duration and shutdown completions remain in place; exact 2025 GET streams have no listen request to complete.
- The closing frame contains no policy or exception data. Request observations retain the underlying cancellation/failure and bounded diagnostic cause; stream/subscription metrics retain their actual close reason. Explicit denial during reconciliation now uses `SUBSCRIPTION_AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` and reconciliation maintenance outcome `DENIED`, while an unsuccessful check retains its failure classification. A physical disconnect, failed writer or write-idle timeout winning delivery retains its transport reason; partially written revoked frames still abort before disclosing more bytes.
- The simulator reserves the server completion reason without canceling its closing frame, preserving both the terminal message and semantic close reason. Its cancellation and capture-limit elections remain authoritative. Public Java signatures are unchanged; authorizer/reason Javadoc and core/website guidance describe the behavior.
- Eight new regressions cover renewal and reconciliation denial/exception, numeric and string listen IDs, clean HTTP termination, exception-free wire data, request/maintenance/close outcomes, capacity reuse, simulator parity, and a disconnect racing terminal reservation. The four initial socket regressions reproduced abrupt EOF before the fix. Two additional transport regressions reproduced the retained-unwritten-hint and partially written-frame gaps against the prior completion behavior. Server-failure completion now atomically removes unwritten notification frames, preserves the acknowledgment and separate terminal reservation even at regular queue capacity, releases payload accounting outside the channel lock, and aborts an already-started notification without writing its remainder. Existing resistant-renewal and same-expiration lease tests now require their correlated terminal plus clean chunk end. Existing reconciliation scheduling and resource catch-up checks now require denial classification and clean completion without fenced hints.

**S5h validation:** the four initial socket regressions reproduced abrupt EOF before the fix; both added transport regressions failed against the prior completion behavior. The final 34 focused closing-frame/authorization/resource-fence tests passed on Corretto 17. All 460 related subscription, task, metrics, transport, lifecycle, localization, simulator and public API reflection tests then passed separately on Corretto 17 and 25 with zero failures, errors or skips. Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed with exactly the same 376 warning path/text entries as S5g and zero new warnings. Offline website API/reference checks passed (303 owners, 2,215 signatures, 74 members and 319 linked types), as did all 18 core-file links. Both repository whitespace checks passed and both indexes remain empty. Raw logs remain under `/private/tmp/soklet-s5h-*.log`, outside Git. This is development evidence, not API-ledger refreeze or immutable-candidate release validation.

**S5i implemented — completed-task result availability (October 5, 2026):**

- **F085 confirmed and fixed:** the published [2026-07-28 Tasks schema](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/ext-tasks/blob/main/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts) requires `CompletedTask.result` in both `tasks/get` and `notifications/tasks`. An authorized task lookup whose originating tool is unregistered or currently hidden now fails its detailed read through the existing HTTP 500 / fixed JSON-RPC `-32603` path before running the sanitizer. Missing and hidden origins receive the same non-disclosing error. Soklet does not change the persisted task, invent a failure state, or claim a terminal completion without output. Explicit later polling can recover after access is restored or on a capable node; the error does not guarantee automatic client retries.
- Advisory subscription projection suppresses that unavailable completed snapshot through the existing bounded failure path. Other eligible task notifications continue. Suppression consumes no terminal delivery; a later change hint or subscription reconciliation can retry after access is restored, without a busy retry loop. Existing generation/lease fences still protect already queued frames. The wire serializer separately rejects any detailed completed snapshot lacking rendered result fields, while lightweight internal authorization and task-creation handles remain valid.
- Seven regression cases reproduced the prior behavior: hidden and removed-origin polling, replacement-node recovery, hidden and removed-origin notifications, a revoked result fenced before offer followed by reconciliation recovery, and simulator parity. They require fixed wire errors, no sanitizer calls on unavailable results, unchanged stored snapshots, healthy peer notifications, and later full-result delivery. The removed-origin test also exercises the serializer guard directly. No application-facing Java signatures changed. Core and website Tasks guidance now describe this behavior. F086 (invalid stored output disposition) remains a separate S6 review.

Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed with the same 376 warning path/text entries as S5h and zero new warnings. #### S5j — operation-scoped metric transition deferral

- Replaced the shared short-transition gate with an internal scope token. Each transition withholds its own provisional metric records; nested scopes on the initiating thread release together. Cross-thread failure cleanup closes the same token, and repeated close is harmless. Request submission, protocol completion, cancellation, deadline reservation, connection admission and transport-failure observation now use those scopes. Server/Soklet start and shutdown retain their separate global lifecycle gate.
- Scope closure signals the existing bounded metrics worker and never invokes a collector inline on the closing protocol/connection thread. Draining inside the current transition also returns without invoking callbacks. Pending provisional failures remain discardable even when another operation drains. A later ordinary record now re-signals an earlier asynchronous delivery requirement, preventing a discarded/empty transition from stranding subsequent metrics.
- Eligible records are serialized in enqueue order; records within a transition preserve their order. Independent eligible operations can pass records still withheld by another transition, so there is no global cross-thread enqueue/causal order claim. Withheld and eligible records share the existing 4,096-record bound. Lifecycle reclamation, discard and delivery also remove the bounded identity ownership marker; queued events still retain no request, context, throwable or application-owned carrier. No application-facing Java type or signature changed. Core guidance, collector Javadoc and website metrics guidance describe the ordering and callback behavior.
- Four initial regressions reproduced the old shared-gate behavior: unrelated delivery paused behind a transition, nested scope delivery blocked unrelated work, closing a scope waited for a blocked/reentrant collector, and an asynchronous attempt followed by provisional discard stranded a later record. Three further regressions cover independent cross-thread scope closure and reuse, repeated close, the separate lifecycle gate, aggregate pending-record bounds and lifecycle reclamation while another transition remains held.

The JDK 25 run used a clean build. This includes all 13 metric backpressure cases, public metric reentrancy/fatal restart, lifecycle, dispatcher cancellation/deadlines, modern/2025 transport, subscription/task policy and simulator coverage. The new capacity fixture was corrected to enqueue both lifecycle records at capacity before draining; its first version freed a slot between those records and made an incorrect two-eviction assertion. Final Corretto 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed with exactly the same 376 warning path/text entries as S5i and zero new warnings, after making the private identity-map field type explicit. Offline website API/reference checks passed (303 owners, 2,215 signatures, 74 members and 319 linked types), as did all 18 core-file links. Repository whitespace checks passed and indexes remain empty. Raw logs, warning comparisons and source backups stay under `/private/tmp/soklet-s5j-*`, outside Git. These are working-tree development checks, not API refreeze or immutable-candidate release validation.

### S6 — MCP application correctness and limits

- Elicitation decimal validation is implemented in S6b. Preserve the supported MRTR value union and declared capability boundaries; do not add unsupported sampling/roots features incidentally.
- Bounded integral conversion and the aggregate expansion budget are implemented in S6c. Estimate converted size before allocation; retain the per-value and per-binding limits for all typed integral targets.
- Tool input/binding error classification by revision is implemented in S6d. November/modern tool calls return a safe `isError` result for tool input validation failures; preserve June behavior and JSON-RPC errors for malformed envelopes/unknown tools. Diagnostics remain bounded and instance-free.
- Fix bounded-executor queue promotion and callback order so releasing one dispatcher slot cannot cause every queued request to fail. Isolate cancel callbacks before ticket interruption/release is delayed by application code.
- Numeric JSON equality/hash codes and documented canonical framework-state round trips (F075/F090) are implemented in S6e. Preserve the reviewed sealing/AAD/canonical security profile; preserving application BigDecimal scale would require a deliberate versioned encoding change.
- Exact-key protection fingerprint identity (F080) is implemented in S6f. Diagnostic encoding `v2` distinguishes HMAC-equivalent raw keys; key bytes, sealing behavior and trace correlation remain unchanged. Compare fingerprint version/profile/value together across the fleet.
- Accepted-request parameter binding and documented round/expiry boundaries (F076/F077) are implemented in S6g. Use bounded canonical headroom without changing transport, response, state-envelope or sealing contracts. Public continuation metadata remains a separate API-review decision.
- JSON string/token accounting and bounded schema-path traversal (F089/F094) are implemented and documented in S6h. Preserve independent ceilings and generic, revision-specific validation errors.
- Make current input/output/string/binary/state limits discoverable and consistent in errors/docs. Validate content/MIME/icon/string values early where the revision requires it. Do not automatically add public constants or tunable JSON ceilings just because the review proposes them.
- Fix clear builder/runtime differences, such as invalid reserved metadata or content accepted until serialization, where they can be folded into existing validation.
- Handler JSON-RPC exception visibility through interception (F070/F063) is implemented and documented in S6q. Preserve the exact original object through the continuation and distinguish its unchanged rethrow from interceptor-authored errors by invocation-local identity.
- Task return/capability classification and persisted invalid-state disposition (F086/F087) are implemented and documented in S6i; task input-client capability projection (F064) is implemented in S6j. Remaining reviews cover localization fallback differences, skill file/directory conflicts and simulator response-head/terminal-frame ordering. Preserve explicit application-policy failure behavior.

**Validation:** typed binding/schema, privacy/redaction, state retry, task capability, content boundary and deterministic simulation race tests. Security-sensitive state changes require the existing state-profile tests and format compatibility review.

### S6a completed — custom handler executor boundaries (October 6)

- Independently reproduced F069 with a one-worker `ThreadPoolExecutor`, `SynchronousQueue` and rejection policy: the exiting worker resubmitted the next ticket before returning to the pool, which rejected and discarded all 128 queued tickets. A separate graceful-drain case discarded already-admitted work after the executor stopped accepting submissions. An inline executor entered application code on the submitting thread. All three small boundary tests failed against the prior implementation.
- Preserve normal executor handoffs. If a promoted ticket's handoff throws `RejectedExecutionException`, the already-accepted worker retains that ticket and its dispatcher slot and executes it iteratively. No retry queue, timer, extra worker or recursive fallback is introduced. Queue ordering, deadline/cancelation entry fences and physical-exit accounting remain in place. Interrupt status is cleared between tickets, and each ticket's own requested interruption is applied at entry. S2d's bounded cancellation-sidecar reservation and observer-before-interruption election ordering are unchanged.
- Detect synchronous inline executor execution before application entry. The guard covers only the active submission call: a single-thread executor may later run an enqueued ticket on the same worker that submitted it. Initial submission rejection returns the fixed HTTP 503/JSON-RPC -32603 capacity response and produces capacity-rejection metrics; rejected handoffs that successfully reuse the worker produce no false rejection. Operation-handler observations retain the exact submission failure without putting its details on the wire. An application handler or policy callback that throws `RejectedExecutionException` keeps ordinary application-failure handling.
- A bounded policy reproducer also showed initial executor rejection escaping as an application exception. Classify only a rejected submission ticket as policy capacity exhaustion. A recovery callback executes afterward; a callback-thrown rejection remains the original exception. Public socket tests cover both handler and catalog-policy rejection/recovery, direct handoff and CallerRuns behavior on modern MCP, 2025-06-18 and 2025-11-25. One fixture correction kept the default catalog policy implicit; explicitly configuring an allow-all policy legitimately adds callback execution and changes the metric/submission counts.
- Source Javadocs, core guidance and the website explain executor ownership, rejection, iterative worker reuse and the unchanged finite bounds. Silent discard policies remain unsupported. Executor decorators must not assume a fresh executor task per invocation; application-owned thread-local cleanup remains application-owned. No supported public signatures changed.

### S6b completed — decimal elicitation (October 6)

- Independently reproduced F074 with four failing regression cases: the internal validator rejected `3.50`, the public response matcher returned false, a real framework-protected retry returned HTTP 400 / JSON-RPC -32602 before handler reentry, and a real task update left the task in INPUT_REQUIRED instead of consuming the decimal answer.
- Verified the [TypeScript schema at the pinned final-specification commit](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/modelcontextprotocol/blob/5f5440bb26a62e2cf3440b92da5a667efa03b267/schema/2026-07-28/schema.ts#L2962): elicitation content permits numbers, including decimals. The vendored generated JSON schema uses an integer branch; that does not override the TypeScript union. The vendored schema and conformance pins are unchanged.
- Replace integer conversion with the codec's existing number serialization preflight, shared through a package-private method. Numeric input remains an exact BigDecimal; validation does not normalize, render, expand to an integer or convert to floating point. Production number/token and exponent bounds remain authoritative. Tests cover decimal and exponent values, both exponent limits, oversized coefficients and extreme BigDecimal scales without large allocations. Nulls, nested objects and non-string array elements remain outside the content union; open extension fields and the deliberate Roots/Sampling exclusions retain their existing contracts.
- The real protected-retry fixture emits a number field with decimal default/minimum/maximum, rejects malformed response shapes and missing current-request capabilities, then completes with the exact `3.50` answer and verified state. The durable-task fixture ignores an invalid union answer, consumes a valid decimal once, does not overwrite it with a duplicate, and permits application-owned task completion afterward.
- Public response matching continues to validate the protocol union rather than the requested form schema. Applications must still validate integer fields, ranges, required fields, action semantics and authorization. Core guidance, source Javadocs and the website state that boundary and the production numeric limits. This is the modern multi-round input path; no new legacy input feature or supported public signature was introduced.

### S6c completed — bounded integral conversion (October 6)

- Independently reproduced F092 with three failing, small tests against the pushed core (`a2169f7e`). A BigDecimal conversion trap proved that oversized exponent values and fixed-width out-of-range values reached integer conversion before rejection; the exact expanded-character boundary was also absent. The trap prevented large allocation, including an extreme negative scale. No reviewer heap-amplification probe or sustained fuzz/load run was used.
- Before `toBigIntegerExact`, check mathematical integrality, the expanded decimal length and any fixed-width Java range. Typed integer input allows at most 1,024 expanded characters including a minus sign. A single JSON-to-Java binding permits at most 4,194,304 expanded integer characters in total. Charge every conversion before integer allocation, including repeated values; nested records, arrays, lists and maps share the budget, and each new binding starts with a fresh budget. Zero and clearly fractional values avoid integer expansion. Oversized individual values retain the sanitized NUMBER_OUT_OF_RANGE reason; aggregate exhaustion has a bounded, instance-free binding-limit diagnostic.
- BigDecimal input remains exact and is not expanded to an integer. Existing integer-ID conversion and legacy cancellation size guards remain in place. Mirrored integer headers now check the safe-integer range before exact long conversion and use bounded long parsing for the header spelling; protected-state integer/long fields check their declared ranges before exact primitive conversion. The state format, canonicalization, sealing/AAD and supported public signatures are unchanged.
- New socket coverage exercises modern MCP and both supported 2025 revisions: exact positive/negative integer boundaries, pre-handler oversized rejection, the production aggregate budget, and a successful later call. The largest request is under 40 KiB and caps expansion accounting at 4 MiB of decimal characters. Unit traps cover rejection before conversion, shared array/map/record budgets, per-call reset, fractional/extreme-scale values and positive finite limit construction. Existing annotation-generated tools use the same synthetic-record binding and retain their runtime/compiler coverage.
- Source guidance and the website document these runtime limits separately from the derived schema. The executable finite-bound inventory now includes the numeric bounds. Its validation also exposed older missing control/metric guards, the removed fixed projection-queue bound and two stale test names; synchronize those entries with current code and its existing regressions. The complete finite-bound source check passes (27 bound groups, 286 source candidates). Update only this slice's changed privacy-source descriptors: numeric capacity record mechanics and the scalar dispatch invariant; their existing conservative diagnostic classifications remain unchanged.

### S6e completed — numeric JSON equality and canonical state (October 6)

- Independently reproduced F075/F090 with six failures in seven small tests against pre-S6e source: scale variants failed numeric/structural equality and a real protected retry failed the handler's equality check after moving to a second Soklet instance. The supplied-value/scale inspection test already passed and remains part of the required contract.
- Public `McpJsonNumber.equals(...)` compares exact numeric values using `BigDecimal.compareTo`; its hash code normalizes the coefficient and combines its hash with the normalized scale. Normalize the coefficient from scale zero and keep the combined scale in a long so accepted extreme BigDecimal scales cannot overflow during hashing. All zero representations share a hash. Neither operation renders or expands an exponent, converts to an integer value or floating point, or changes the retained BigDecimal. Numeric leaves propagate this contract through public JSON object/array equality and hash collections. This is a deliberate equality behavior change for 4.0; supported public signatures are unchanged.
- Preserve the existing canonical JSON bytes, plaintext versions, sealing/AAD, binding and key derivation. Framework state continues to remove trailing zeros and normalize zero: `100` opens as `1E+2`, `1.50` as `1.5`, and `0.0` as `0`. JSON-tree equality and hash lookup now survive that normalization. Original decimal scale/spelling is not encoded; identifiers and exact decimal text belong in JSON strings. Source Javadocs distinguish result inspection from verified retry state, and MCP guidance, the migration guide and the website explain exact numeric conversion/compareTo, toPlainString's formatting boundary, and ordinary JSON getValue scale retention.
- Regressions exercise equivalent positive/negative/zero/exponent spellings, unequal values/types, object insertion and array ordering, nested hash-set/map lookup, retained supplied values, both extreme BigDecimal scales, a bounded coefficient/scale matrix and a conversion/rendering trap. A fixed canonical-byte vector proves that the security representation is unchanged while the restored public tree is equal with the same hash. Two independent listeners with the same keyring and authorization partition exchange state through two retries; a same-prior-ID retry is rejected before handler entry, canonical numeric leaves and exact string fields are checked, and the original state remains unmodified.
- The broader selection exposed two stale bootstrap assertions from completed S3 changes. Update tests to the existing F107 exact normalized endpoint-path contract and F109 blank-client/nonblank-server implementation contract. These updates change no production behavior. The existing decimal-elicitation regression still checks the exact `3.50` BigDecimal, so numeric equality cannot mask loss of ordinary input scale.

### S6f completed — exact protection-key fingerprint identity (October 6)

- Independently reproduced F080 in two bounded regressions against pre-S6f source: a 32-byte key versus that key followed by a zero byte, and a 65-byte key versus its SHA-256 digest. Direct HMAC gives equal tags in each pair, as expected from [RFC 2104](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc2104.html#section-2). Real sealing with identical context, plaintext, activation prefix and nonce produces different ciphertext; both crossed opens fail. Despite that incompatibility, the old diagnostic fingerprints matched. Both regressions failed at the fingerprint assertion after checking the sealing mismatch.
- Correct protection diagnostics only. Extract a separate fingerprint key as `HMAC-SHA256(SHA256("soklet-mcp-key-fingerprint-extract-v2\0"), rawKeyBytes)`, keeping the raw key as the HMAC message, consistent with the extraction structure in [RFC 5869](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5869.html#section-2.2). Authenticate the existing length-framed ID/profile/role metadata with the derived key and `soklet-mcp-key-fingerprint-v2\0`; aggregate sorted entries under `soklet-mcp-keyring-fingerprint-v2\0`. Explicit temporary raw-key and derived-key copies are wiped by their existing ownership/finally paths. Stored master keys and all sealing derivation, envelope, nonce, plaintext and AAD behavior remain unchanged.
- `McpProtectionKeyringFingerprint.VERSION` and `getVersion()` now report diagnostic encoding `v2`; `PROFILE` and `getProfile()` still report `soklet-mcp-protection-v1`. No supported public signature changed. Trace fingerprints and tokens retain their existing frozen vectors. An independent Python HMAC/SHA-256 construction first matches the prior v1 golden, then supplies the pinned v2 golden `JXu-Td1vDi5VOz0j_tqs2oFOGGKM_p6sbWRRfBTbE1A` for the same reviewed ring. Verification insertion order remains irrelevant.
- Regressions cover both collision families in active and verification-only roles, unchanged caller key bytes, same-ring equality and cross-instance opens, public snapshot/diagnostic projection, independent trace identity, and convergence after removing the differing keys. A shared active key alone does not produce convergence while differing verification material remains. Retained snapshots stay immutable during rotation.
- Source Javadocs, MCP/security guidance, migration guidance, the security profile, rotation runbook and website explain comparing version/profile/value together. ### S6g completed — request-state parameter bounds and continuation limits (October 6)

- Independently reproduce F077 in two binding regressions and two real-listener cases against pre-S6g source. Parameters larger than 4 MiB parse under the accepted request profile, but binding fails at the response-output limit. Both the default 10 MiB and configured 16 MiB listeners invoke the handler and replace its initial input-required result with a sanitized HTTP 500. A third public test already passes against the prior implementation and establishes the current final-round behavior.
- Change only canonical parameter validation: request-state binding uses a package-private profile sharing the existing accepted-task-argument limits, with the 16 MiB input ceiling, 32 MiB canonical output headroom and unchanged production depth/node/token/scalar limits. Canonical spelling can grow: a bounded fixture with 90,000 `1e-6` leaves and sixteen one-million-character strings fits the 16 MiB wire limit but exceeds it after canonical rendering as `0.000001`. An independent length-framed SHA-256 construction checks every canonical byte, including bytes after 16 MiB. Preserve filtering, domains, length framing, binding/AAD, plaintext and encryption; existing exact vectors remain unchanged. No supported public signature changes and no transport, response or state-envelope limit increases.
- Two independent production-keyring listeners exchange small state with stable arguments larger than 4 MiB, and then larger than the default 10 MiB under the configured 16 MiB limit. Changed stable data beyond the response ceiling fails before handler entry; an identical retry completes on the second instance. The protected continuation remains small because its binding retains the fixed-size parameter digest, not a copy of the arguments.
- Verify and document F076 under its planned documentation disposition. The round limit counts emitted continuations; a valid final-round retry may complete but cannot emit another framework continuation. A real listener verifies the sanitized `-32603` / uncommitted HTTP 500 re-emission failure after handler execution and then successful completion using the unchanged incoming state. Strengthen the existing deterministic-clock regression to open one nanosecond before expiry, then reject both open and re-emission at exact expiry without calling the seal adapter. Framework round, issuance and expiry remain hidden from the public state accessor. Exposing them is a separate public API review, not implemented by this slice. Application counters/deadlines, durable consumption and idempotency are documented; seal failure does not roll back side effects or make side effects atomic with sealing.
- Update source Javadocs, MCP/security guidance, the security profile and website. Correct F091's website input/output conflation: input follows the default 10 MiB/configurable 16 MiB body limit; output remains 4 MiB and structural/schema/binding limits are independent. Record the shared canonical-parameter bound in the existing finite-JSON group and its production-value test. Correct required owner ordering and the new test mapping after the initial inventory checks exposed those omissions. The final reviewed finite-semantics digest is `82c743152d1f1570d8615120ef289d82acadc1cb56cc57b97c4b1feceed210cc`.

- Verify F089 without changing the frozen ceilings. Only diagnostics snapshot full paths, with their copy charged before allocation. Sibling-path, immutable-diagnostic and exact operation-boundary tests retain the diagnostic contract.

### S6j completed — task polling input capabilities (October 6)

- Independently reproduce F064 with real listeners and the public simulator. Nine of 15 new tests fail before the change because unsupported elicitation modes escape in successful task snapshots. The matrix covers form, URL and mixed outstanding requests; absent, empty-object, single-mode and both-mode capabilities; repeated requests requiring the same mode; and subsequent capable polls. Unknown and unauthorized task reads are already indistinguishable and disclose no input requirements.
- Revisit the archived September 10 review's R12-4 recommendation to remove polling checks. The current [elicitation specification](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/client/elicitation#capabilities) forbids sending unsupported modes and requires per-request capability declarations. The [Tasks specification](https://github.com/modelcontextprotocol/ext-tasks/blob/main/specification/2026-07-28/tasks.md#task-update-requests) uses the same input requests and ordinary elicitation trust rules, while successful polls must include all outstanding requests. Reading these together, adopt the capability-safe interpretation: reject an incompatible whole poll rather than send unsupported requests or filter the snapshot. The task's `resultType: "complete"` discriminator does not authorize unsupported input delivery. This is a local implementation disposition, not an official conformance result.
- Reuse the existing subscription capability validator immediately after an authorized, origin-validated task lookup. Missing outstanding modes return HTTP 400 / `-32021` with only deduplicated missing capabilities. No task ID, request key, message, URL, schema or partial snapshot is sent. Unknown/unauthorized IDs retain their neutral error before this check, and invalid persisted declarations retain S6i's internal-error classification. No task state is changed or transition published by failed or successful reads.
- Preserve complete delivery to capable clients and require only actual outstanding modes, not unused origin declarations. Empty `elicitation: {}` supports form only. Capabilities from creation or earlier polls are not remembered; simulator downgrade/upgrade polls verify that boundary. Working and completed tasks can be read without elicitation support. Existing creation, update, cancelation, task notification and deferred-output behavior remain covered by related regressions. Update the old exact-wire test that asserted unsupported polling delivery, and document polling behavior in core, security, website and manager Javadocs. No public Java signatures change.
- Inventory review finds one new request-access descriptor for consulting the current polling capabilities. Classify it under the existing application request/context boundary with the new negative and authorization canaries; update the expected privacy digest only for this reviewed addition. The pre-existing 146 unclassified production descriptors remain S9 work. Finite-bound coverage remains 27 groups / 286 candidates.
- Validation: 148 tests in 20 classes pass on JDK 17 and JDK 25. Offline JDK 21 static compilation passes with the same 376 normalized warning path/text entries as S6i (none added or removed). At the first differing component, `/a/b/{rest*}` also outranks `/a/{id}/c/{rest*}` for `/a/b/c/file`; aggregate placeholder/literal counts no longer choose between these varargs routes. Fixed declarations remain preferred and preserve their existing fewer-placeholders/more-literals rule. Equally specific overlapping declarations still fail; literal spelling, placeholder names and registration order do not manufacture a tie-break. HTTP verbs and HTTP/SSE registrations remain independent.
- A package-private immutable specificity key is shared by runtime selection, resolver ambiguity grouping and processor ambiguity detection. No supported public Java signature changes. The default processor still discovers/validates declarations at compile time and emits a lookup table; ordinary HTTP/SSE runtime resolution and reflective handler invocation remain as before. Explicit class/method registration and generated-index registration apply the same ordering.
- Eight new regressions cover the review's catch-all/nested-prefix examples, component-position precedence, a longer prefix competing with an earlier literal, fixed-route precedence, genuine ambiguity, disjoint literals and HTTP/SSE partitioning. Compile-time controls accept the newly ordered varargs overlap and retain ambiguous fixed/varargs diagnostics. A real `javac` fixture emits the index and a fresh bounded JVM selects and invokes the same handlers through all three public resolver factories, without contaminating another test's singleton. Before correction, the catch-all misroute and incorrect positional ambiguity were reproduced in runtime resolution and processor diagnostics.

### S7b completed — incremental HTTP/SSE route indexes (October 7)

- **F264:** the processor merges newly compiled HTTP/SSE declarations into one authoritative prior snapshot: current CLASS_OUTPUT first, then the enabled sidecar and persistent caches only when the output snapshot is absent. An empty output snapshot remains authoritative. Older caches can no longer resurrect a route after its annotation is removed. Touched owners are replaced, and retained owners absent from the compiler's sources/classpath are removed. Untouched source-visible and compiled owners, nested types with legal dollar-sign identifiers, and dependency JAR owners remain eligible. The MCP endpoint index keeps its existing behavior.
- Compiler visibility is the boundary: a deleted source whose obsolete classfile remains visible still resolves. Builds must remove obsolete class output or run clean after deletion/renaming; the processor does not infer source ownership from arbitrary filesystem layout. The existing optional `soklet.pruneDeleted` filter remains stricter and checks CLASS_OUTPUT classfile presence. No supported public Java signatures or processor options change. Javadoc, migration guide and website explain snapshot precedence, owner visibility and this clean-build requirement.
- Selected snapshots are read into a temporary map and published only after a complete successful read. Malformed or unreadable selected snapshots fail compilation with the snapshot location and fixed diagnostic text, preserve their bytes and existing caches, and do not fall back to older metadata. Invalid unselected caches are ignored and replaced from valid current output. Failed compilation preserves prior metadata. Failed cache updates invalidate the stale snapshot; inability to update or invalidate is a compiler error. Atomic-write temporary files are removed on failure. The nullable private index-parser result is explicitly annotated.
- Twelve new tests use real incremental `javac` invocations across no-cache, sidecar and persistent modes, with bounded fresh-JVM resolver/invocation checks. Coverage includes deletion/rename, clean output, removed final annotations, authoritative empty output, source-visible untouched types, nested names, dependency JARs, failed compilation, selected/unselected malformed snapshots and blocked cache writes. JDK 21 static-analysis clean compilation passes with **361 warning occurrences / 135 unique normalized entries**, zero additions and two existing nullable-return warnings removed. Website API/source-link and protocol-profile checks pass; finite derivation remains **27 groups / 286 candidates / 40 reviewed exclusions**.

The processor catches the declared argument failure and emits its reason with a fixed fallback for a null message. Invalid declarations are not collected, and a failed incremental compilation preserves the last good index. Javadoc, migration guide and website explain the shared rules and rebuilding invalid older declarations.

### S8a completed — process ownership and listener liveness (October 7)

- **F226:** built-in HTTP accept/connection loops and the SSE listener now explicitly use non-daemon threads. A directly started server keeps the JVM alive after `main` returns. Complete shutdown releases all those threads; custom transports retain responsibility for their own liveness. Default HTTP application/timeout workers and the SSE timeout worker explicitly remain daemon regardless of the creating thread. No public Java signatures or process-exit policy change.
- **F225/F293/F227:** the two SSE website examples now use `SokletApplication.run(config, ShutdownTrigger.ENTER_KEY)`. Container/service guidance uses the runner without the stdin trigger. Core Javadoc and migration examples use real JVM registration calls with blocking `soklet::close` for manually owned hooks. Plain `awaitShutdown()` only waits, while `shutdown()` publishes asynchronous intent. The stdin guidance describes active runner registrations sharing the process reader, and EOF leaves the runner/signal hook active; it does not claim the reader's physical lifetime is solved (F222 remains open).
- **F228/C010/C012/F221:** Javadoc, migration and website guidance distinguish serialized daemon transition observers from inline request/transport callbacks. Returning from shutdown/run does not join transition delivery; required cleanup uses bounded `ShutdownCleanup`, with an application delivery barrier when observer state is needed. JVM hooks run concurrently without guaranteed ordering; coordinate logging/resource hooks. Post-run/finally code is unreliable during signal-driven JVM exit. Calling `System.exit()` from work being drained can exhaust shutdown budgets. The runner does not select a signal exit status.
- **F223/F224/F241:** run Javadocs now distinguish canceled startup from actual startup/hook-registration failures. `Soklet.fromConfig` claims transport identities during construction; the application factory defers that claim until run. All public run entry points and the core factory document `TransportOwnershipException`. Docker/Linux's default 10-second stop timeout is shorter than the default 18-second running shutdown; migration/site guidance gives explicit 40-second examples and explains how to recalculate them with cleanup, reporting, other hooks and reserve.
- Seven new subprocess/factory regressions cover HTTP and SSE main-return liveness, aggregate listener release on complete close, runner and manually owned blocking hooks on SIGTERM, ENTER_KEY EOF retaining signal shutdown, and HTTP auxiliary daemon policy. The corrected pre-slice fixtures reproduced **four failures with three passing controls** on JDK 21. ### S8b completed — startup failure cause and rollback parity (October 7)

- **F237:** synchronous built-in startup failures establish local rollback intent before recording their cause. A delegated built-in returns the throw promptly and defers owner termination proof until the owner's shutdown phase. Transparent, termination-owning and nested HTTP/SSE decorators preserve the real bind cause instead of synthesizing premature termination. Earlier independent failures remain controlling, and termination proof still requires physical cleanup.
- **C009:** the MCP runtime now retains whether its elected first startup failure came from the synchronous start call or an independent listener callback. The cause and origin are elected together under the existing lifecycle lock and passed through a distinct internal startup-failure signal. Complete bind/registration rollback within grace is `GRACEFUL_TERMINATION` while startup remains `FAILED`. Independent pre-readiness faults, after-readiness faults and their competing-failure ordering retain their existing unexpected classification. The core unwraps MCP's checked-I/O adapter only when the wrapped cause is the exact recorded startup cause; an application-owned `UncheckedIOException` keeps its original identity. Supported public Java signatures are unchanged; the new signal is on the internal MCP bridge, not a new application SPI.
- **F238:** `TransportRuntime.start` and `TransportTerminationSignal` Javadocs, the migration guide and server-configuration website explain synchronous throws versus independent failure signals. Shutdown phase methods must be safe after partial startup and publish proof only after owned activity ends. Termination-owning decorators also drive their child's cleanup, whose independent proof remains required. A complete cleanup disposition does not imply startup reached readiness.
- **14 new regressions** cover nine occupied-port modes (direct, transparent, termination-owning and nested HTTP/SSE plus MCP), custom synchronous/independent failures, application I/O wrapper identity, and delegated proof ordering/earlier failure retention. The original 11 public fixtures reproduced **seven failures with four passing controls** on pre-slice production source. Existing MCP bind and post-bind subscription tests now explicitly assert graceful rollback and absence of fabricated unexpected termination. **95 selected tests across nine classes pass on JDK 17, 21 and 25**, zero failures/errors; four SSE cases skip on JDK 17 and none on 21/25. The suites include first-cause races, failures between runtime/common readiness, proof barriers, fresh-owner resource reuse, and three existing privacy canaries.

Website API/source-link checks pass (**303 owners, 2,215 signatures, 74 members, 319 linked types and 18 source links**). Source-only privacy comparison of seven touched production files changes **1,265 to 1,267 descriptors**: the two internal startup-signal Throwable declarations are attributed to the existing exact application-carrier boundary (`PRIV-BOUND-006`) with cause-identity canaries. ### S8c completed — late custom startup compensation (October 7)

- **F232:** freezing a direct participant now retains its original forced deadline independently of whether a phase worker has entered. When the still-running configured HTTP/SSE `start()` eventually returns or throws, that same tracked startup worker delivers one best-effort forced shutdown call. Deferred graceful shutdown is subsumed, no cleanup worker or fresh budget is allocated, and queued/repeated phase calls cannot replay compensation. The active-at-freeze fact remains the classification authority; late resource closure/proof does not turn an already-published `TERMINATION_UNKNOWN`/`INCOMPLETE` result into a complete result.
- A compensation failure after classification freeze is contained without adding suppressed failures to a published cause or replacing frozen evidence. A blocking compensation remains on the existing daemon lifecycle worker; it cannot delay an already-completed shutdown stage or reopen admission. Custom runtimes still own their resources, must honor startup cancelation, and must keep phase methods prompt. The simulator retains its existing no-compensation freeze behavior; this change applies to the configured direct transport runtime. Supported public Java signatures are unchanged. `TransportRuntime` Javadoc, migration guidance and the server-configuration website explain the fallback and its limits.

### S8d completed — residual worker liveness and stdin retirement (October 7)

- **F220 / F172:** Soklet-created streaming callback, termination-observer, cleanup-diagnostic and supervisor workers, MCP platform application/policy workers, cancellation callbacks, protocol request processors and timers now have explicit daemon policies. Noncooperative residual application work cannot alone keep the JVM alive after the standalone runner reports incomplete shutdown. Executor bounds, physical termination checks, residual retention and frozen-result classification are unchanged: daemon status is not termination proof, and application cleanup remains skipped after an incomplete core shutdown. Built-in HTTP/SSE/MCP listener threads continue to retain process liveness until closed; custom transports and supplied executors own their policies. No process exit or halt calls are added. Supported public Java signatures are unchanged.
- **F222 / F305:** the shared ENTER_KEY listener now reads one byte at a time without decoder/read-ahead buffers and checks active registrations before starting another read. It stops at the first LF or CR; EOF after unterminated text emits the fixed warning without inventing shutdown intent. Removing the last registration prevents line draining, with the explicit portable limitation that one already-pending byte read may complete before the daemon listener retires. It never closes process-owned stdin; a trailing LF after CR remains available. Registration snapshots and generation epochs are captured together under the listener monitor, preserving concurrent registration, callback-time handoff and launch-failure takeover. Javadoc, migration guidance and the server-configuration website state the exact stdin contract; production-readiness guidance explains daemon residuals and listener liveness.

The child fixtures require real incomplete evidence, skipped cleanup and natural exit without releasing their blocked callbacks or calling process exit. **236 selected tests across 20 classes pass on JDK 17, 21 and 25**, with four expected feature-dependent skips on 17 and none on 21/25. ### S8f completed — independent SSE read deadlines and quiet EOF (October 7)

- **F202: queue wait and application handshake handling share one budget; header reading/parsing pause it.** The handler deadline begins at admission, saves its remaining duration before reading, and resumes that remaining duration after successful parsing. Queue wait is not refunded, and the request-header timeout remains independent even when the configured handler timeout is shorter or equal. A private deadline monitor and generation token prevent an already-delivered canceled queue timer from claiming the channel or interrupting later application work. No callback, channel write or interruption runs under that monitor. Very large handler durations retain the timeout scheduler's saturation behavior.
- Partial-header read expiry returns the existing built-in **408** and reports `REQUEST_READ_TIMEOUT` once, without synthetic internal/read/write/task errors. Idle read expiry closes quietly. Queue expiry remains a finite **503**, now classified through `REQUEST_HANDLER_QUEUE_FULL` rather than an internal connection error; parsed application expiry remains `HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT`, a 503, and cooperative interruption. Physical worker and shutdown proofs are unchanged. No public signature changes: there is no 408-specific `ResponseMarshaler` method, and `forUnparsedRequest(UnparsedRequest)` currently covers other standard-HTTP parsing rejections rather than this SSE pre-Request timeout path.
- **F203: EOF before complete headers is a typed, quiet client disconnect.** The reader throws a fixed-message `EOFException`, and the handshake owner closes without an internal-error event, transport-failure metric or fabricated 500. This covers both zero-byte TCP health checks and partial-header EOF. Complete malformed headers retain their 400/parse-failure behavior, and genuine read errors retain the existing error path.
- **13 new regressions** cover shorter/equal/longer deadline orderings, idle expiry, zero-byte and partial EOF, completing headers after the handler budget would otherwise expire, application interruption, queue expiry with no later resource entry, preserved queue-time deduction, stale deadline callbacks in both phases, and malformed-header controls. The original 11-case socket suite reproduces **eight assertion failures** against pre-slice production, with three controls passing. A broader run exposed a pre-existing initializer fixture race: its one-entry queue could legitimately overflow when live broadcast arrived before catch-up drained. A compiled-class overlay of pre-slice production reproduces that failure. The fixture now drains catch-up before broadcasting, retaining its ordering and retired-unicaster assertions; no initializer production behavior changes in this slice.
- **128 selected tests across 13 classes pass on JDK 21 and 25.** JDK 17 reports **124 tests with 36 expected feature-dependent skips**, all remaining 88 passing; disabled dynamic factories explain the smaller reported count. JDK 21 static-analysis clean compilation passes with unchanged **359 warning occurrences / 133 unique normalized entries**, zero additions/removals. Builds are serial, offline and bounded to two processors and 512 MiB; no fuzz/soak runs. Website API/source-link checks pass (**303 owners, 2,215 signatures, 74 members, 319 linked types and 18 source links**). Javadocs, migration guidance and website configuration/SSE/production guidance describe the independent budgets and quiet closes.

Classifications, matchers and reviewed exclusions are unchanged. Finite derivation remains **27 groups / 286 candidates / 40 reviewed exclusions**. Full S9 source/artifact privacy and lifecycle-harness closure remains pending; final refresh must include the new deadline tests, corrected initializer fixture, changed source hashes and earlier pending scopes without weakening physical proof requirements.

### S8g completed — expanded unparsed-request response customization (October 7)

- Extended the existing `ResponseMarshaler.forUnparsedRequest(UnparsedRequest)` and `LifecycleObserver.didRejectUnparsedRequest(UnparsedRequest)` paths. The only public signature additions are four `UnparsedRequestReason` enum constants: `REQUEST_READ_TIMEOUT` (default 408), `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` (413), `UNSUPPORTED_CONTENT_ENCODING` (415), and `REQUEST_BODY_DECOMPRESSION_FAILED` (400). There is no new 408-specific marshaler method or builder hook.
- Standard HTTP partial request-line/header/body/trailer read expiry now permits a custom response; zero-byte idle expiry still closes quietly. Aggregate overflow before a trustworthy request line now produces an eligible unparsed 413. URI/UTF-8 validation, unsupported method/content coding, and malformed compressed-body failures before `Request` construction also route through the hook. Decompressed size/ratio and other size violations that can produce a valid `Request` retain `forContentTooLarge(...)`.
- SSE malformed input, target/header limits, partial read expiry and untrusted-line aggregate overflow now use the same pipeline. Unsupported SSE expectations retain the existing 400/MALFORMED_REQUEST policy. EOF before complete headers remains quiet. Admission overload, shutdown, broken-socket/write and post-commit stream failsafes continue to work independently of application response generation.
- Extracted the existing bounded application pipeline and finite serializer into package-private `UnparsedRequestResponseSupport`. Observation precedes marshaling, both stay off selectors and timeout-wheel threads, and late responses cannot replace the fallback. Later HTTP validation failures retain their original handler deadline; SSE rejection handling uses the handler budget left after queue wait. Rejection fallback delivery does not prove termination of application code that ignores interruption: the new real-wire shutdown test freezes INCOMPLETE until the underlying executor actually terminates.
- HTTP parser failures and SSE handshakes retain at most 64 KiB, excluding following pipelined input. Partial timeout captures stop at received input. Later HTTP construction failures report the exact observed wire count with an empty, truncated capture instead of retaining a second raw copy of normal requests. The shared finite serializer preserves transport-owned close/length/date framing, bounds the whole response to 64 KiB and rejects streaming/file-backed/oversized output. Removed the obsolete private serializer reflection seam from its test.
- Updated API Javadocs, migration guidance and the website response-writing, server-configuration, SSE and production-readiness guides. The 24 added cases cover real custom statuses, callback order/threading, headers and post-processing, byte attribution, capture truncation and pipeline boundaries, parsed-size routing, invalid/throwing/null output, timeout fallback/late-output suppression and physical residual-worker evidence.
- **Local validation:** 208 selected HTTP/SSE/parser/deadline/lifecycle tests passed on JDK 21, followed by the additional HTTP validation-timeout case; 209 passed on JDK 25. JDK 17 reported 197 tests, with 48 expected skips and all 149 executed cases passing. The JDK 21 static-analysis clean compilation passed with the same 359 warning occurrences / 133 unique warnings as S8f. Builds remained serial, offline and limited to two processors / 512 MiB.

### S8h completed — SSE initialization outcomes and HEAD handling (October 7)

- Independently reproduced **F204/F207/F211** before changing production code: 12 of 13 bounded cases failed on the prior implementation. Live HEAD invoked the event source and returned a 500 with content; OPTIONS advertised HEAD; initializer exceptions/overflow lacked the documented termination callbacks; capacity, handler, response-preparation and accepted-head write failures lost their establishment classification or cause. The explicit application-rejection control passed.
- SSE routes now use **GET only**. HEAD receives a bodyless **405** through the existing `ResponseMarshaler.forMethodNotAllowed`, without event-source/initializer invocation or lifecycle admission. OPTIONS advertises GET and OPTIONS. Custom/failsafe and parsed-timeout HEAD responses also suppress content. HEAD/OPTIONS do not require a free stream slot. Standard HTTP retains its HEAD-to-GET fallback and route-observation behavior.
- After an accepted 200 is written, initializer exceptions and overflow now preserve the lifecycle's elected **PRODUCER_FAILED/BACKPRESSURE** reason and original cause, including caught overflow. The handshake worker delivers `didEstablishSseConnection`, `willTerminateSseConnection`, and `didTerminateSseConnection` in order, once each, to observers and metrics collectors; failed initialization never activates a broadcaster. It no longer emits a synthetic INTERNAL_ERROR connection rejection, handshake failure, or REGISTER_ERROR transport metric. Healthy initialization keeps its existing activation timing. Reservation/work retention and physical shutdown proof remain intact.
- Before acceptance is written, processing, response-preparation and writing failures report **INTERNAL_ERROR with the actual cause**; explicit application rejection remains HANDSHAKE_REJECTED. Both fast connection-cap and authoritative lifecycle-cap rejection now report **CAPACITY_EXCEEDED**. The only public API addition is `SseConnection.HandshakeFailureReason.CAPACITY_EXCEEDED`; no callback or marshaler signature changed. Throwable propagation in `HttpRequestResult` is package-private and omitted from its public rendering/equality.
- **17 new regressions** cover simulated/live HEAD, Allow headers, custom and failing marshalers, custom/failsafe timeout responses, HEAD/OPTIONS under exhausted connection/lifecycle capacity, checked initializer failure, escaping and caught queue overflow, observer/metrics pairing, both capacity boundaries, handler failure, explicit rejection, forbidden accepted framing, deterministic head-write failure, and complete cleanup. Existing HTTP HEAD, initializer/shutdown, parsing/framing, unparsed customization and deadline regressions remain in the bounded validation selection.

Builds are serial/offline with two CPUs and 512 MiB; no long fuzz run or soak. JDK 21 static analysis passes with **358 warning occurrences / 132 unique warnings**, no new diagnostic; one existing nullable-request diagnostic was removed.
- Javadocs, the migration guide and website SSE/configuration guidance match the chosen behavior. Website source-contract and core-link checks pass offline. ### S8j completed — SSE capacity and duration contracts (October 7)

- Reproduced **F217** with **8 failures in 18 bounded cases**, zero errors, before changing production code. Positive sub-millisecond heartbeat intervals and overflowing heartbeat/retry values were accepted; the normal framing, independent capacity, valid-boundary and negative-value controls passed.
- Heartbeat intervals must now be at least **one millisecond**, with a whole-millisecond value representable in a Java `long`; invalid values fail at server `build()`. `SseEvent` similarly rejects retry overflow at event construction. Retry remains non-negative, including zero and positive fractions below one millisecond; serialization discards fractions, while `getRetry()` retains the supplied duration. No arbitrary upper timeout or new disable convention was introduced.
- **F212:** retained the intentional **256-lifetime** default and independent **8,192-connection** cap. Updated builder Javadocs, migration guidance and both website SSE/configuration guides to explain the smaller effective ceiling, retained initialization/cleanup, and that `concurrentConnectionLimit(0)` disables only the connection cap. Lifecycle admission follows application handshake handling and precedes accepted headers/initialization; the method may run before a capacity 503. The existing real-wire tests verify both admission boundaries and initializer suppression. Connection-required work belongs in the initializer.
- **F219:** preserved legal event-only, ID-only and retry-only payloads. Javadocs and public guides now explain that browser `EventSource` requires a data field for message dispatch and that `.data("")` delivers empty data. Verified against the [WHATWG field/dispatch rules](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/server-sent-events.html#event-stream-interpretation). ### S8k completed — simulated SSE reading and bounded delivery (October 7)

- Reproduced **F250** before changing production code: **10 of 12 cases failed** (eight assertion failures and two connection-already-terminated errors). Event-only and comment-only listeners disconnected from accumulating unobserved payloads; full retained capture prevented even the first live delivery; reentrant callbacks could terminate their own connection. The existing unread-client and genuinely full mixed-delivery controls passed.
- The first event or comment consumer now starts simulated reading before draining captured payloads. New payloads of unregistered types are discarded after that point. Initializer output and broadcasts captured before reading retain their original per-type ordering and remain available to a later consumer. Late registration does not replay payloads discarded while that type was unobserved. No public Java type or signature changed.
- Capture before reading and pending deliveries to registered consumers have **separate combined limits**, each equal to `connectionQueueCapacity`. Retained capture cannot consume live delivery capacity; both registered payload types still share one delivery limit. Up to one capacity of captured payloads and one capacity of pending deliveries can coexist, excluding consumer calls already in progress. These are count bounds, not byte bounds. A client without any listener can still exhaust capture; a blocked registered listener can still cause BACKPRESSURE. Close/teardown clear both buffers, while physical consumer work retains its existing reservation proof until return.
- **12 new cases** exercise 200 mixed broadcasts with either listener type, mixed initial/pre-registration capture, late registration, reentrant callbacks, full capture alongside blocked live delivery, registration of the other type under load, shared live backpressure, capture overflow without listeners, connection release, and physical residual-worker accounting. Existing initializer ordering, consumer error routing, keyed providers, isolation and shutdown regressions remain in the selection.

JDK 21 static analysis passes with the unchanged **358 warning occurrences / 132 unique warnings**, with no new diagnostic. ### S8l completed — simulated SSE connection observation (October 7)

- Reproduced **F251** against the prior implementation: all **nine initial cases failed** (eight assertion failures and one missing-snapshot error) because SSE connection callbacks were absent. The simulator now invokes the existing `LifecycleObserver` and `MetricsCollector` establishment and termination hooks. Each accepted lifetime uses one immutable `SseConnection` metadata snapshot carrying the dispatch request, exact resource method, establishment instant and application client context. No public Java type or signature changed.
- Accepted initializer failures still pair establishment with termination. Both terminal hooks and collectors receive the same elected `StreamTermination`, preserving its original reason/cause and monotonic duration measured at election. Initializer failure is elected before invoking a potentially blocking establishment observer; a concurrent shutdown cannot replace that outcome. Shutdown before initializer entry suppresses application initialization but still pairs logical acceptance with termination.
- Framework release clears the delivery buffers and broadcaster registration before publishing one terminal observation job on the existing bounded coordinator executor. Observation waits for establishment callbacks to finish, runs outside lifecycle/registry locks, and remains physically accounted for until return. `close()` stays nonblocking with respect to application observers. A blocked observer retains lifecycle capacity, cannot prevent another admitted lifetime from reporting termination, and can produce incomplete teardown with residual work until it actually exits. No new executor, public setting or retention allowance was introduced.
- Failed handshakes use the coordinator's separate bounded rejection-observation allowance. Explicit rejection and finite request refusal report `HANDSHAKE_REJECTED`; retained request-processing failure reports `INTERNAL_ERROR` with its original cause; stream admission refusal reports `CAPACITY_EXCEEDED`. These outcomes have no accepted connection lifecycle. Exhausted observation allowance omits the notification with a log event while preserving the finite response. Observer and collector failures are isolated and logged independently with the exact application Throwable. Typed delivery intentionally emits no socket-write callbacks or wire metrics; public documentation states that limit.
- **13 new cases** cover metadata/identity/order, repeated close, checked initializer failure, initial and broadcast queue overflow, scope shutdown, observer/collector failure isolation, blocked terminal observers and independent progress, retained admission capacity, shutdown during establishment, first-failure election, cancellation before construction, bounded failure observation, and physical work after incomplete teardown. **122 selected tests pass on JDK 21 and 25**; JDK 17 reports **113 tests, 18 expected skips, 95 executed passes**. JDK 21 static analysis passes with the unchanged **358 warning occurrences / 132 unique warnings**, with no new diagnostic. ### S8n completed — lifecycle budgets and diagnostic channels (October 7)

- Documented **C013** on `SokletApplication`, in migration guidance and on the website: the standalone `soklet-terminal-report` goes directly to stderr captured at runtime creation, bypasses observers/`LogEvent`, and is attempted for runner primary failures, failed/timed-out startup, forced/incomplete shutdown, unexpected component termination and failed/timed-out cleanup. Complete forced shutdown still triggers reporting. Output stays capped at 16 KiB of valid UTF-8; failure details are class names without messages, stacks or cause/suppressed traversal. One shared 250-millisecond finalization allowance contains reporter failure/stall, including diagnostic preparation; a blocked output worker may remain and cannot replace the lifecycle result. No new reporter configuration or runtime behavior was introduced.
- Documented **F233** in `LifecyclePolicy` and the deployment/migration guides. Zero means an immediate deadline for each of the four phases, never unlimited waiting or disabled supervision. Zero startup can time out immediately; zero startup cancelation leaves no allowance before shutdown phases; zero grace moves unproven participants to force; zero force leaves no allowance for physical proof. Both shutdown budgets zero can produce incomplete shutdown even on an idle running listener. The review's universal wording is not the contract: the waiter checks its predicate before checking the deadline, and evidence already available at classification can establish completion. Existing accepted zero values and termination-proof rules are preserved.
- Documented **F246** on `LogEvent`, its Throwable accessor, the four affected event constants, migration guidance and website event-logging/MCP-observability guides. HTTP/SSE logging, typed MCP callbacks, application-created events, generic HTTP 2025 session GET/DELETE callbacks and aggregate lifecycle-transition failure events retain their separate application-owned contracts. No attachment, cause identity or redaction behavior changed.
