### S8 — lifecycle, SSE and simulator behavior

- Correct the `awaitShutdown`/hook migration guidance and website samples as part of the first lifecycle change. Document JVM-hook concurrency, signal exit codes, cleanup location, Docker/Kubernetes stop budgets and post-`run` code limitations.
- Decide transport-owned thread liveness explicitly and consistently. Recommend retaining process liveness for a directly started server, rather than making ordinary `start(); return` silently stop working. Keep observer/control sidecars bounded and daemon where appropriate; verify complete cleanup does not leak a non-daemon keeper. Do not add `System.exit`/`halt` to the library.
- Keep request deadlines active through graceful drain. S6a prevents MCP custom-executor inline application entry; verify the remaining HTTP/SSE selector and interruption paths separately. Preserve deadline/termination-group proofs rather than masking residual work.
- Preserve actual startup failures through decorators; align direct and delegated termination classification. Verify late custom-start completion receives a best-effort forced stop without altering an already-published result or launching unbounded cleanup work.
- Correct observational callback examples instead of making arbitrary `didStop*` hooks critical shutdown work. Verify duplicate logging; recommend moving default stderr logging to the default observer implementation and making the OTel observer non-logging, preserving sensible out-of-box diagnostics.
- SSE: separate header-read/handshake deadlines, classify zero-byte health-check close quietly, give initialization/capacity/head-write outcomes accurate reasons, and make HEAD behavior valid without an erroneous 500 body. Check memoized provider failure sharing and duration boundaries.
- Simulator: do not let an unconsumed SSE payload type silently kill an otherwise-reading test. Define activation/registration semantics, preserve bounded capture, and make real termination observable. Fix MCP terminal-before-head ordering with deterministic barriers rather than thousands of random race iterations. Keep documented capture limits and isolation decisions.
- Review C008's graceful HTTP-feed policy and F239's custom SSE SPI access as the public-API questions below.

**Validation:** short subprocesses for normal main return, SIGTERM, ENTER_KEY EOF, startup bind failure/decorators, blocked observer, bounded cleanup and residual work. Add one aggregate HTTP+SSE+MCP shutdown test with a shared short deadline. Exercise HTTP/SSE HEAD and rejection behavior on real sockets and compare supported simulator behavior.

### S9 — metrics, public contracts and published guidance

- Repair misleading streaming request lifetime/byte/status observations and missed MCP subscription maintenance. Prefer existing carrier/result types over a parallel public stream event hierarchy.
- Fix histogram snapshot/reset invariants and duration-sum overflow behavior with an explicit unit/API compatibility decision; merely relabeling nanoseconds as seconds is invalid. Preserve Prometheus/OpenMetrics contract and stable label meanings.
- Correct case-insensitive simulator headers, routine termination vocabularies and GET/DELETE route labeling where those changes are verified.
- Update source Javadocs and soklet.com together with each code slice; compile changed examples. Include proxy Host setup, session/stateless limitations, client version selection, servlet buffering limits, streaming context/thread boundaries, content limits and actual observer semantics.
- Remove obsolete internal checkpoint narratives from public README/MCP/SECURITY/CHANGELOG and website prose. Git history already preserves tracked obsolete prose; do not create another checked-in archive of screenshots/raw runs. Keep a concise, dated interoperability statement; state tested capabilities accurately while release is still pending.
- Review and reconcile the pre-existing privacy-source inventory drift before full release-matrix closure; preserve the conservative classifications and canary evidence rather than accepting an unreviewed derived scan.
- Recompute API freezes/Javadocs and affected consumer compatibility after API decisions settle. Do not freeze/pin every intermediate slice or rewrite already-staged sibling changes.

Independently constructed `HistogramSnapshot` objects continue to retain the caller-supplied count.

### S9c completed — histogram sum range and boxed snapshot API (October 7)

- **F291 diagnosis:** the pre-change histogram and actual default subscription exports freeze the sum at `Long.MAX_VALUE` while later observations increase count. Bounded probes reproduced this on JDK 17, 21 and 25. At a hypothetical steady 5,000 aggregate completed stream-seconds per wall-clock second, the modeled ceiling is **21.350398233 days**; this is workload-dependent arithmetic, not a long-running soak. Four initial regression cases reproduce **three failures, no errors**, with the quiescent-reset control passing.
- **Approved contract:** the user selected nonnull `Double` sums, boxed scalar snapshot inputs/results and `List<Long>` constructor buckets. The shared histogram uses `DoubleAdder`; the exporter serializes the floating sum without narrowing it to `long`. This covers duration, byte and queue-depth histograms. Original units, metric names, label meanings, integer counts, boundaries, min/max and percentile values are retained. Duration families still contain nanoseconds. No new public type, setting, unit selector or parallel sum accessor is added.
- Sums are approximate operational metrics. Small additions to a large total can round away, and accumulation order can affect the result, as described by [DoubleAdder](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/26/docs/api/java.base/java/util/concurrent/atomic/DoubleAdder.html). The one-second increment near the former ceiling is represented by a 999,999,488-nanosecond delta. This is not an exact-accounting contract. The approved range change does not rescale or rename metric families.
- The public constructor defensively copies both bucket lists into private primitive storage. All arguments/elements must be nonnull, list sizes must match, and the sum must be finite and nonnegative; negative zero becomes `0.0`. Independently supplied count semantics are preserved. A private primitive/array constructor avoids List boxing during internal histogram capture. Public scalar getters and indexed/percentile inputs use the approved reference types; zero remains a present value. Existing percentile behavior and S9a's count/final-bucket equality and weak whole-snapshot/reset contract remain intact.

**Actual public declarations**, verified from source, compiled bytecode and generated Javadoc (signatures only):

```java
public static final class HistogramSnapshot {
    public HistogramSnapshot(
        @NonNull List<@NonNull Long> bucketBoundaries,
        @NonNull List<@NonNull Long> bucketCumulativeCounts,
        @NonNull Long count,
        @NonNull Double sum,
        @NonNull Long min,
        @NonNull Long max);

    @NonNull public Integer getBucketCount();
    @NonNull public Long getBucketBoundary(@NonNull Integer index);
    @NonNull public Long getBucketCumulativeCount(@NonNull Integer index);
    @NonNull public Long getCount();
    @NonNull public Double getSum();
    @NonNull public Long getMin();
    @NonNull public Long getMax();
    @NonNull public Long getPercentile(@NonNull Double percentile);
    @Override @NonNull public String toString();
}
```

- This changes **nine existing declarations**: the constructor and eight numeric methods. The `toString()` signature is unchanged, and its implementation accepts floating sums. No public primitive/array snapshot overload remains. Updated six constructor fixtures and sum text assertions in the existing MCP aggregation tests. Source Javadocs, the migration guide and website metrics guidance explain the source/binary break, null/finite requirements, copying, precision and unchanged units. No application-example Java fence changed.
- **Thirteen new regression cases** cover post-ceiling histogram/export growth in both formats, exact reference types and null annotations, defensive copying and independent count, null lists/elements/scalars/accessors, nonfinite/negative sums and negative-zero normalization, mismatched lists, percentile boundaries/overflow, full integer precision, two concurrent writers, quiescent reset/immutable prior snapshots, empty snapshots and diagnostic rendering. Existing suites retain byte/depth, filter/reduced-output, histogram-count and HTTP/SSE/MCP coverage. **74 selected tests pass on JDK 21 and 25**; JDK 17 runs **73 successfully with the one existing JDK-21-or-newer SSE-network test skipped**. No failures or errors. The runtime adds no reflection; public API reflection is test-only.
- JDK 21 static analysis passes with unchanged **358 warning occurrences / 132 unique warnings**, with no new diagnostic. The intended long-to-double recording conversion is explicit. ### S9e completed — subscription-maintenance counters and snapshot API (October 7)

**Pre-change diagnosis:** F132 was confirmed in the default collector. The runtime already emits `McpMetricsEvent.SubscriptionMaintenance` through the bounded asynchronous delivery path, and custom collectors can observe it. The OTel collector already records it as `soklet.mcp.subscription.maintenance` with endpoint/work/outcome attributes. The prior `DefaultMetricsCollector.didRecordMcpMetricsEvent` had no maintenance branch and `McpMetricsSnapshot` had no corresponding aggregate. The prior Prometheus/OpenMetrics exporter consequently lost these events.

A bounded public-API probe exercises all **21 existing work/outcome combinations twice**, delivering **42 events per collector**. On **JDK 17, 21 and 25**, a fresh default collector still returns the shared empty MCP snapshot and both exports remain byte-identical to their pre-event outputs. Repeating with a live subscription also leaves both exports unchanged; the existing active gauge remains one. A custom-collector control receives all 42 events. This is diagnosis against current production classes, not a future implementation test or a long-running qualification. Probe source/classes/logs remain outside Git under `/private/tmp/soklet-s9e-review`.

**User-approved and implemented addition:** one immutable `McpMetricsSnapshot.SubscriptionMaintenanceKey` with endpoint/work/outcome dimensions, a snapshot count map, and its builder setter. Reuse the existing `McpMetricsEvent.SubscriptionMaintenance.Work` and `.Outcome` enums. Use the existing `fromDimensions` key factory and map-builder naming patterns. This type applies to maintenance across supported revisions, so it has no 2025 prefix. Keep the aggregate key separate from the occurrence event, consistent with the other snapshot keys, so future event payload additions cannot silently become aggregation dimensions.

**Exact implemented public additions** (signatures only; all existing declarations omitted):

```java
public final class McpMetricsSnapshot {
    @NonNull
    public Map<@NonNull SubscriptionMaintenanceKey, @NonNull Long>
        getSubscriptionMaintenance();

    public static final class Builder {
        @NonNull
        public Builder subscriptionMaintenance(
            @Nullable Map<@NonNull SubscriptionMaintenanceKey, @NonNull Long>
                subscriptionMaintenance);
    }

    public static final class SubscriptionMaintenanceKey {
        @NonNull
        public static SubscriptionMaintenanceKey fromDimensions(
            @NonNull String endpointPath,
            McpMetricsEvent.SubscriptionMaintenance.@NonNull Work maintenanceWork,
            McpMetricsEvent.SubscriptionMaintenance.@NonNull Outcome maintenanceOutcome);

        @NonNull public String getEndpointPath();
        public McpMetricsEvent.SubscriptionMaintenance.@NonNull Work getWork();
        public McpMetricsEvent.SubscriptionMaintenance.@NonNull Outcome getOutcome();
        @Override public boolean equals(@Nullable Object other);
        @Override public int hashCode();
        @Override @NonNull public String toString();
    }
}
```

This is **one new public type and nine new methods**: seven key methods and the getter/setter. Domain values are references, with `Long` counts; the standard `Object.equals`/`hashCode` overrides retain Java's required primitive returns. It is additive and introduces no new application callback or required implementation method. The user approved this exact surface with “Lgtm. Proceed.” Source and reflection checks verify the actual declarations, generic types, null annotations and parameter names; `javap -public` verifies the key exports seven methods and no public constructor. Removing the new key and two map methods from the current Phase 6 nullability manifest reproduces the previous SHA-256 exactly. The 33 added canonical lines are limited to this approved addition; the regression digest is updated without changing preserved API signature snapshots.

**Contract to implement after approval:**

- `getSubscriptionMaintenance()` returns an immutable, defensively copied map. Its default is empty. Builder null/empty input clears the map, null keys/values fail, negative counts fail, and explicit zero counts remain valid, matching existing count-map semantics. Keys require a nonempty endpoint path and nonnull work/outcome, compare/hash all three dimensions, and redact the endpoint in `toString()`. Framework event paths come from configured endpoint registrations; manually supplied dimensions remain application-owned.
- Record one count per delivered maintenance event. Counts describe observed outcome events, including coalescing and stale-result discards; they are not unique subscriptions, unique started attempts, durations, or active-work gauges. The existing work vocabulary is `AUTHORIZATION`, `CATALOG_PROJECTION`, `RECONCILIATION`; outcomes are `SUCCEEDED`, `DENIED`, `TIMED_OUT`, `CAPACITY_REJECTED`, `FAILED`, `COALESCED`, `STALE_RESULT_DISCARDED`. No new enum value, synthetic RPC method or protocol-version label is added.
- Store counters in the existing `ConcurrentLruMap`/`LongAdder` pattern with the current **8,192-key map capacity**, yielding bounded retention and the existing eviction/weak snapshot behavior. Include the map in the MCP empty-snapshot decision, build/copy path and quiescent counter reset. Preserve existing live-gauge/reset bookkeeping. Immutable earlier snapshots retain their copied counts.
- Add **`soklet_mcp_subscription_maintenance_total`**, a counter with exactly `endpoint`, `work`, `outcome` labels. Work/outcome use the existing lowercase `Locale.ROOT` convention; no request IDs, subscription IDs, URIs, principals or exception text become dimensions. Both formats use their existing counter metadata/serialization/filter rules. Emit observed nonzero series independently of subscription-open flags, so maintenance-only observations are visible without inventing a lifecycle gauge. A reset clears these counters. This adds one family, bringing the documented MCP family count to 23.
- Update source Javadocs, migration guidance, website metric-family/API references and their new type/member links together. OTel already handles the event; no parallel OTel change is needed for this defect. Reconcile only the new privacy/source/finite-bound delta. API freeze and candidate provenance updates follow the remaining public API decisions, rather than each interim slice.

**Validation and scope:**

- Before production changes, three direct-event/actual-runtime export regressions reproduce **three failures, no errors** on JDK 21. A separate earlier probe reproduced all 42 dropped observations on JDK 17/21/25. The actual-runtime checks now delegate delivered events into the default collector before publishing test barriers; authorization, catalog projection and reconciliation outcomes appear in both snapshots and exports.

- **60 selected metrics/runtime tests across seven classes pass on JDK 21 and 25**. JDK 17 executes **59 successfully, with one existing JDK-21-or-newer SSE network case skipped**; no failures or errors. **47 additional API ownership/reflection/observability tests pass on JDK 21**. Current owner/key/getter/builder expectations now include the approved addition; the earlier test explicitly freezing maintenance as nonaggregated is corrected. The same contract run exposed an old fingerprint scalar expectation: align it with the already implemented and documented S6f diagnostic encoding `v2`; protection production code and its profile are untouched.
- JDK 21 static analysis passes with unchanged **358 warning occurrences / 132 unique warnings**, no new diagnostic. The configured JDK 26/offline links generate updated public Javadocs without diagnostics; all nine new member anchors are verified. Website source-contract/core-link checks pass (**304 current owners / 303 preserved snapshot owners / 2,215 preserved signatures / 74 checked members / 320 linked types; 18 core links**). No website Java example fence changes.
- Add only the **12 newly derived privacy descriptors**: nine MCP snapshot/key surfaces under their intentional exact application boundary, one redacted diagnostic renderer, and two fixed-message Throwable constructions. New canaries cover exact dimensions, copies, invalid input diagnostics and redaction. Candidate count is **7,503**; the earlier **143 unclassified production candidates and 61 stale production descriptors** remain unchanged for S9 reconciliation. Finite-bound verification remains **27 groups / 286 candidates / 40 exclusions**. The bounded retention regression checks the collector map directly.
- Builds are serial/offline, with two CPUs and 512 MiB; no fuzz or soak run. Source backups, probes, canonical nullability manifests and raw run logs stay outside Git under `/private/tmp/soklet-s9e-*`. New edits are unstaged/uncommitted; existing core/site/OTel indexes are preserved. This is local remediation evidence, not immutable candidate validation. The owner inventory includes the current type while the signature ledgers remain preserved; final API refreeze/pinning follows the remaining public decisions.

### S9f completed — HTTP response stream metrics (October 7)

**Before implementation:** F288 applied to `HttpServer` responses using `MarshaledResponse.stream(...)` / `streamingResponseBody(...)`. The default collector removes request state in `didFinishRequestHandling`, records the handoff duration, and records `getBodyLength()` (zero for these streaming responses). HTTP and simulator terminal delivery invokes only the lifecycle observer. OTel's metrics collector follows the same handoff/body-length path. The existing lifecycle callback handles finite replacements and unadmitted rejection observations as well, so forwarding every lifecycle termination to metrics would incorrectly count rejected original streams as admitted responses.

A bounded real-socket public-API probe on **JDK 17, 21 and 25** pauses an 11-byte producer after its first write and waits for the actual metrics handoff callback. The stream is unfinished while `activeRequests=0` and recorded body size is zero. Releasing the producer yields both payload segments and `COMPLETED` lifecycle termination; the default body size stays zero and its request-duration aggregate stays identical to the earlier handoff snapshot. This is a diagnostic reproduction, not validation of a fix. The probe and logs stay in `/private/tmp/soklet-s9f-review`; no production or test source changes are made for this review.

**User-approved public addition:** one default terminal callback, an immutable termination-count map on the existing snapshot/builder, and one key composing the existing `HttpServerRouteStatusKey` with `StreamTerminationReason`. This is **one new public type and nine methods**: one callback, two map methods, and six key methods. No new event hierarchy, public constructor, per-response setting, abstract SPI requirement, establishment callback, separate active-stream gauge or second request-duration histogram is proposed.

The callback includes `requestDuration` because `StreamTermination.getDuration()` describes only the stream's lifetime. They are distinct scopes: collectors need the full request duration without retaining their own start timestamp or including asynchronous observation delay. `responseBodySizeInBytes` is boxed `Long`; it describes observed output and is never inferred from how much a producer queued. These APIs are shared HTTP facilities, not tied to a legacy MCP revision.

**Exact approved additions** (declarations only; existing declarations omitted):

```java
public interface MetricsCollector {
    default void didTerminateResponseStream(
        @NonNull StreamingResponseHandle streamingResponseHandle,
        @NonNull StreamTermination streamTermination,
        @NonNull Duration requestDuration,
        @NonNull Long responseBodySizeInBytes) {}

    final class Snapshot {
        @NonNull
        public Map<@NonNull HttpResponseStreamTerminationKey, @NonNull Long>
            getHttpResponseStreamTerminations();

        public static final class Builder {
            @NonNull
            public Builder httpResponseStreamTerminations(
                @Nullable Map<@NonNull HttpResponseStreamTerminationKey, @NonNull Long>
                    httpResponseStreamTerminations);
        }
    }

    final class HttpResponseStreamTerminationKey {
        @NonNull
        public static HttpResponseStreamTerminationKey fromDimensions(
            @NonNull HttpServerRouteStatusKey httpServerRouteStatusKey,
            @NonNull StreamTerminationReason streamTerminationReason);

        @NonNull public HttpServerRouteStatusKey getHttpServerRouteStatusKey();
        @NonNull public StreamTerminationReason getReason();
        @Override public boolean equals(@Nullable Object other);
        @Override public int hashCode();
        @Override @NonNull public String toString();
    }
}
```

Interface member classes are implicitly public/static. The new key is final, immutable and thread-safe, with a private constructor; its factory requires both existing immutable dimensions. Equality/hashCode use both dimensions; diagnostic rendering redacts the embedded route/status key and exposes only the fixed termination reason. Snapshot counts are boxed `Long`. Null/empty maps clear, null keys/values and negative counts fail with fixed diagnostics, explicit zeros are retained, and builder inputs/returned snapshots are defensively copied. The proposed declarations compile on JDK 17 in a temporary copy of the actual `MetricsCollector` source with declaration stub bodies; `javap` confirms six exported key methods and no public constructor. This validates declaration syntax and footprint, not runtime behavior. Supported production APIs remain unchanged.

**Proposed runtime/metric contract:**

- Emit the metrics callback once for an admitted streaming response actually handed to the HTTP transport, including an admitted stream that ends before its producer enters. The existing `didFinishRequestHandling` callback still describes application handling/handoff. Its exact original request identity and actual offered response are preserved. Publish the new terminal callback after that metrics handoff callback, even when transport termination wins the race; use a constant-size private per-dispatch record rather than correlating caller-controlled request IDs. Deliver metrics before potentially blocking application lifecycle observers, using the existing bounded admitted-stream notification infrastructure. Exceptions remain contained as `METRICS_COLLECTOR_FAILED` and cannot change transport results.
- Capture the full `requestDuration` at the transport's terminal transition using a monotonic clock and the same processing-start origin as the existing `didFinishRequestHandling` duration. Transfer this timing privately through the built-in result/transport bridge. It includes handler and stream time and excludes waiting to deliver the terminal notification or later physical cleanup after logical termination. Keep `StreamTermination`'s stream-only duration scope; no new getter on that shared carrier is required.
- For production HTTP, count payload-buffer bytes accepted by `SocketChannel.write`. Exclude headers, chunk lengths, delimiters and the terminal chunk. Record partial payload progress even if a later framing/payload write throws, and freeze the total at termination under the existing progress/termination synchronization. Bytes accepted into the producer queue but never written do not count. The result does not claim client receipt or application consumption. The built-in simulator counts bytes accepted into its bounded materialized output, including accepted prefixes before failure/limit rejection; it cannot claim real network delivery. Both values are nonnegative and zero is a real observation.
- The default collector retains streamed request state after handoff, removes the originating request thread's thread-local reference promptly, and decrements `soklet_http_requests_active` only at terminal metrics delivery. Record one full request-duration sample and one observed body-size sample at that transition. Preserve the earlier handler-duration and approximate TTFB observations. Finite responses keep their existing representation-length behavior. Quiescent reset clears cumulative values and preserves active request state/gauges; earlier snapshots remain immutable. No unbounded completed-request history is added.
- Add `soklet_http_response_stream_terminations_total` with exactly `method`, `route`, `status_class`, `reason` labels. Reuse the existing HTTP route/status label projection; `reason` uses the fixed uppercase enum names consistent with other HTTP/SSE reason labels. Store counts using the existing 8,192-key `ConcurrentLruMap` / `LongAdder` pattern, expose them through the new snapshot map, and apply the existing counter metadata/filter rules in both formats. A producer failure after a committed 200 remains a 2xx response with `PRODUCER_FAILED`, rather than inventing a wire 500. Manual keys/snapshots remain intentional application-owned exact-value carriers; built-in labels never include request IDs, request targets, query/session values, cause messages or authored exception classes.
- Pre-commit finite replacements (505/503/etc.), HEAD/bodyless suppression and other unadmitted streams remain finite HTTP observations using the actual offered status/body length. Existing lifecycle notifications can still retain the original rejected stream. They do not trigger the new admitted-stream metric or fabricate a successful 2xx stream; no duplicate request-duration/body samples or active-gauge decrements are allowed. Gate on the actual transport disposition, not a reason enum that could also describe an admitted stream.
- Update OTel metrics to defer its HTTP streamed request terminal samples to the new callback, use supplied full duration/body size, preserve existing instrument/naming strategies, and expose a bounded Soklet stream-termination counter. Runtime/simulator changes, core and OTel regression coverage, Javadocs, migration guidance and website documentation move together. Dedicated SSE-server and semantic MCP callbacks keep their existing ownership; the proposed change fixes `HttpServer` response streams and does not recount those streams as HTTP responses. Legacy GET/DELETE HTTP observation finishes at its own transport transition already; preserve S9d route attribution and its controls.

**Implementation verification after review:** reproduce failures in committed-source regression tests, then cover a live stream between handoff/completion, exact complete and partial payload counts, framing exclusion, producer failure/client disconnect/timeouts/server stop, early termination versus handoff, actual finite rejection statuses, source/protocol/capacity/producer-executor rejection, HEAD/bodyless suppression, bounded callbacks, throwing/blocked observer isolation, simulator success/partial-failure/limit behavior, exact custom callback identity/order/boxed inputs, reused request IDs and concurrent streams, duplicate terminal delivery, reset with active streams, immutable maps and new key redaction, both text formats/filters/capacity, OTel naming strategies and the S9d legacy HTTP controls. Use serial/offline focused JDK 17/21/25 runs with two CPUs/512 MiB, then static analysis, API/Javadoc/site checks and only the new privacy/finite-bound delta. No fuzz/soak or interim release pin/refreeze is needed.

**Approval and implementation:** the user approved these exact declarations and semantics with “Sounds good, do it.” The one new public key and nine methods are implemented. A package-private per-dispatch observation record carries the monotonic processing origin and orders terminal delivery after metrics handling finish; no public carrier getter or ID correlation is added. Built-in socket accounting tracks payload-buffer position deltas even when a later write fails, freezes at the logical terminal transition under write synchronization, and excludes HTTP framing and unwritten queued bytes. Simulation serializes bounded output appends against termination and retains accepted-prefix observations on failures/limits. Finite replacements/suppression keep finite accounting. The default collector retains admitted active request state while clearing the handoff thread's reference, then samples once at termination. OTel keeps only active stream state by physical request identity and defers its existing terminal HTTP instruments under both naming strategies; its new termination counter has four fixed dimensions. Metrics run before lifecycle stream observers. The older OTel early-terminal span fixture now gates lifecycle handling finish, allowing metrics handling finish to advance first while retaining its early span-termination control.

**Validation:** a new source regression fails before the fix with an unfinished stream already absent from the active gauge; after implementation it stays active and records all 11 payload bytes at completion. Twenty new tests cover the API/map/key contract, live/early termination ordering, exact/partial payload output and framing exclusion, producer failure, disconnect, timeout, forced server stop, finite HTTP/1.0/HEAD and capacity rejection, simulator success/failure/limit prefixes, duplicate signals, reused IDs, live reset, concurrent termination, both formats/filters and the 8,192-key bound. The selected 17-class core suite reports **146 tests on JDK 17/21/25**: zero failures/errors, with one existing SSE test skipped on 17. It also retains S9d legacy controls, S9e maintenance metrics and existing stream race/observer/rejection coverage. The OTel suite reports **62 passing tests on each JDK 17/21/25**, including both naming strategies and unchanged span controls. These are local development checks using a temporary artifact built from the current core source, not exact-commit release gates.

Static analysis succeeds with the unchanged **358 occurrences / 132 unique diagnostics** and no new diagnostics. Direct JDK 26 Javadoc generation reports zero diagnostics and all nine new member anchors are checked. The source contract inventory passes with **304 MCP owners / 91 non-MCP owners / 395 total current types**; preserved signature ledgers remain unchanged. The approved shared-host nullability delta contains only 14 added canonical lines: removing them reproduces the prior Phase 4 digest exactly. The website reference check passes (304 current MCP owners, 303 snapshot owners, 2,215 preserved entries, 74 checked members, 320 linked types); all 18 core blob links pass their local intended-file preflight. Source Javadocs, migration guidance, metrics website and OTel documentation move together.

The privacy scan's current-source candidates move from 7,503 to 7,532: 65 internal-signature rekeys preserve their earlier classifications and 29 new candidates are reviewed in the appropriate generic callback, exact log/carrier, explicit Throwable and redacted diagnostic boundaries. Earlier unclassified/stale drift is unchanged; this is a delta review, not full privacy closure. The finite-bound inventory still passes with 286 candidates and 40 exclusions. Logs, probes, artifact and backups stay in `/private/tmp`; existing staging is preserved. No commits, pushes, staging, fuzz/soak, refreeze or release pinning.

### S8p completed — combined HTTP, SSE and MCP shutdown (October 8)

- Added two real-socket regressions in `CombinedTransportShutdownTests`, with application work active concurrently in all three built-in transports under one Soklet owner. They verify that HTTP, SSE and MCP receive the **same exact absolute deadline** for each delivered shutdown phase. HTTP/SSE use test-only forwarding probes of their existing internal operations; MCP's received deadline is read under its existing lifecycle lock. The configured built-in identities, normal shutdown operations, physical proof checks and residual-evidence paths are preserved. No production behavior, reflection path or public Java signature changes.
- Graceful shutdown cancels the accepted SSE connection/initializer with `SERVER_STOPPING`, while the admitted finite HTTP writer and MCP tool remain un-interrupted and retain their response paths. Releasing them delivers the complete eleven-byte HTTP payload and final chunk, plus the successful MCP JSON result. All three components report `GRACEFUL_TERMINATION`, with no residual evidence and no forced-phase invocation.
- The forced case holds an HTTP producer, SSE initializer and MCP handler that deliberately ignore interruption. Closing the SSE socket does not prove its initializer returned. The shared 300-millisecond graceful and forced budgets freeze an `INCOMPLETE` aggregate result, with **positive `RESIDUAL_ACTIVITY` evidence for every component** while each worker remains physically running. After release, actual HTTP/SSE executors and the MCP runtime barrier converge, but repeated shutdown/await/close preserve the original result identity and evidence. Late convergence is observed with a separate bounded test wait; MCP's original owner phase deadline is never extended. SSE termination observation may follow initializer exit.
- **Both tests pass on JDK 21 and 25**, with zero failures/errors/skips. The entire source/test compilation also succeeds on JDK 17; both combined live-SSE cases are intentionally skipped there because the built-in live SSE transport requires virtual threads (JDK 21+). Builds are serial/offline and limited to two processors and 512 MiB; no fuzz or soak run. Production source and the finite/privacy derivation roots are unchanged, so this slice introduces no production inventory delta. Local probes, backups and raw logs remain outside Git under `/private/tmp/soklet-s8p-implementation`.
- A separate Java compiler probe in an external package reproduces F239: `HttpRequestResult.getSseHandshakeResult()` cannot be accessed by a custom SSE transport. At completion of S8p, the exact recommendation below was prepared for public-contract review; the user subsequently approved it and S8q implements it. New changes remain unstaged/uncommitted; existing core/site/OTel index entries are unchanged. This closes the planned aggregate shutdown verification gap, not all remaining S6/S8/S9 checks or immutable candidate validation.

**Next at S8p:** settle the custom SSE handshake accessor (F239) and graceful HTTP-feed signal/policy (C008), then remaining verification and privacy closure/S10. The combined tests preserve finite draining; they do not give an indefinite HTTP producer a new graceful-stop signal or declare 4.0.0 release-ready.

### S8r completed — graceful HTTP-feed state (October 8)

The user selected the smaller recommendation: one boxed state check on `ResponseStream`, with bounded application waits. The complete public addition is:

```java
@NonNull
Boolean isGracefulShutdownRequested();
```

- Live HTTP and simulator graceful-phase delivery now seal admission and publish an advisory flag to each still-live admitted producer, including queued producers. It does not cancel or interrupt work, abort resources, close output, elect a terminal result, alter response/idle deadlines or start the cleanup timer. Finite sources retain draining; a cooperating writer stops taking new items, writes final bytes and returns normally through managed finalization and wire drain. State is per execution/generation and monotonic once requested. Completed/canceled production is not marked; a force-first election prevents a late graceful mark while force is still delivering other cancelation hooks.
- The complete supported public footprint is **one abstract interface method**. Custom `ResponseStream` implementations must implement it. No new public type, callback/registration method, enum value, setting or executor. The earlier callback proposal is superseded in the approved contract below. Application feeds use bounded waits and retain cancelation checks; an ignored request can still exhaust the owner's original graceful budget. Automatic InputStream/Reader/Publisher bodies retain finite draining.
- Added **eight focused regressions**. Four public tests outside `com.soklet` check the exact boxed/nullability/abstract declaration, simultaneous real-wire feed and finite download delivery, producer-thread finalizer tails, normal simulator completion and fresh/reused finite body executions. Four controlled coordinator tests check queued production, admission-only sealing, unchanged cancelation/interruption/cleanup, completed/canceled/fresh state and a force-first race that holds one hook before force reaches another reservation. The queued case retains its existing physical obligation; no new work is admitted after graceful sealing.
- Extended the existing combined HTTP/SSE/MCP graceful shutdown test with an additional cooperating HTTP-feed connection. It receives its final record and final chunk while finite HTTP and MCP remain retained and SSE closes; the exact shared absolute phase deadline, full finite responses and graceful component results remain intact. The existing forced case still freezes positive residual evidence for noncooperative work in all three transports and preserves the result after late physical cleanup.
- **153 selected cases pass on JDK 21 and 25** with zero failures/errors/skips. JDK 17 reports **153 cases: 151 passes and two expected skips** for the combined live-SSE cases requiring JDK 21+. The selection covers the eight new regressions, shared ownership/output/finalization, coordinator accounting/timeout races, publisher behavior, response body factories, HTTP stream terminal observations and 38 existing MCP reflection/nullability contract cases. All eight new cases execute and pass on every JDK.
- JDK 21 static-analysis compilation passes with unchanged **358 warning occurrences / 132 unique warnings** and no new diagnostics after excluding source line references, including embedded references shifted by the new field. Targeted JDK 26 public Javadocs finish with zero diagnostics and the new member anchor resolves. The current japicmp report verifies unchanged **398 API owners (304 MCP + 94 non-MCP)**. Historical signature records and existing MCP nullability digests are preserved; this is current owner/signature evidence, not a final refreeze.
- Updated `ResponseStream` Javadocs, migration guidance, the response-writing and production-readiness website guides, and the permanent literal-fence compiler. **All 28 selected website fences compile against the current local core artifact**, including the new complete graceful-feed example. The two changed pages parse; the MCP API-reference source-contract check and 18 core-blob link checks pass. A temporary jar assembled from existing javax adapter classes supplies the unchanged Servlet example dependency; no downstream dependency pin or published artifact changes.
- Source privacy candidates remain **7,533**. Two internal constructor-signature descriptors are rekeyed under their existing exact application carrier boundary, with public/runtime canaries added. Earlier unclassified/stale source drift is unchanged; full privacy closure remains pending. The finite inventory passes unchanged with **286 candidates / 40 exclusions**.

### S9h completed — servlet response buffering boundaries (October 8)

### S9o completed — simulator SSE response and terminal ordering (October 8)

- **F134:** `McpSimulationRuntime.complete(...)` now accepts a terminal result before publication of its SSE response head. It captures the frame in the existing bounded staging queue, counts its bytes/item once, and defers the stream termination until `acceptResponse(...)` publishes the head and moves the ordered frames into visible capture. Capture-limit failures use that same response-publication boundary and retain their exact item/byte reason. The invariant exception that previously lost the result is removed. Network transport and public Java signatures are unchanged.
- A staged terminal is not yet delivered. `isTerminalWritten()` now requires a published SSE response, excluding an abandoned channel replaced by a finite response. An abrupt runtime end can still discard an unpublished completion and its staged frames; a published completion retains its winner. Existing client-cancel/no-message and typed capture-limit behavior remain covered.
- Three deterministic admitted-tool regressions, for **2025-06-18, 2025-11-25 and 2026-07-28**, pause the first helper-thread progress report after stream opening but before response publication. The handler returns and physically exits in that window. Neither head, frames nor completion are exposed early; releasing the report yields the SSE head, progress, terminal result and exactly-once completion/observations without throwables. All three cases failed with the original implementation. Four new capture tests cover inclusive byte/item budgets, terminal duplication and late close, exact overflow reasons, abort before publication versus completion after publication, and a finite response replacing staged SSE output. Separate boundary controls reproduced the premature delivered-state reports before correction.
- Local validation: **65 selected tests pass separately on JDK 17, 21 and 25**, with zero failures, errors or skips. The selection includes public simulator operations, legacy sessions/GET delivery, Tasks, capture bounds, cancellation and graceful/forced lifecycle barriers. Offline MCP API/reference (**74 member targets**) and core-file link (**18 links**) checks pass. The capture fuzz model only completes published SSE responses and needs no expectation change; sustained fuzzing stays with CI nightly.
- All five core/site/OTel/servlet indexes are preserved and repository whitespace checks pass. New changes remain unstaged/uncommitted. #### Approved and implemented — graceful HTTP feed shutdown (C008)

The user approved the smaller recommendation on October 8: a state check with bounded application waits. S8r implements the complete public addition on the existing `ResponseStream` interface:

```java
@NonNull
Boolean isGracefulShutdownRequested();
```

The earlier callback design is superseded. There is no graceful callback, registration, diagnostic enum addition or extra executor. Custom `ResponseStream` implementations must implement this one abstract method. Existing cancelation, body factory, transport attachment and shutdown policy signatures are unchanged.

**Contract:**

- The method is thread-safe; output and resource operations remain producer-confined. State belongs to one response execution and runtime generation. It begins false; graceful shutdown sets it for still-live admitted production, including queued producers, and it stays true thereafter. Completed/canceled production receives no new request. Sealing admission alone does not request completion. Forced election suppresses a later graceful request even while force is still signaling other reservations.
- The request is advisory. It does not cancel the token, interrupt the producer, abort resources, close output, discard bytes, change configured deadlines, elect a terminal reason or start the cleanup clock. Finite downloads may ignore it and continue draining. A feed checks between items, uses bounded waits comfortably within the shared graceful budget, writes final output and returns normally; owned encoders then finalize and queued output drains.
- Client disconnects, response/idle deadlines and forced shutdown remain authoritative, including during final output. Producers keep checking cancelation. `getDeadline()` still describes the configured response deadline. A request alone does not prove successful delivery or physical termination. Normal completion reports `COMPLETED` and can yield aggregate graceful termination; ignoring the request can still exhaust the graceful budget.
- Automatic InputStream, Reader and Publisher bodies retain finite draining. An indefinite source needs application-managed completion or a writer that observes this state; Soklet does not infer feed intent or automatically truncate those sources.

**Implementation:** the shared coordinator atomically seals admission and marks eligible reservations under its existing lock. A per-generation forced flag prevents late graceful marking after force wins. A volatile per-reservation flag provides cheap state polling, including after the captured execution ends. Managed streams use the reservation state for both live HTTP and simulation. HTTP graceful-phase delivery requests completion separately from the admission-only helper used by force; simulator HTTP graceful delivery follows the same path. No application code runs while signaling and no callback work, timer, queue or worker is added.

**Cost:** one required public interface method, one signal flag per reservation, a generation force marker and an internal state supplier. Application feeds must bound their waits. The existing owner's absolute grace boundary and physical-work accounting remain unchanged.

**Verification:** the new external-package API/runtime tests cover boxed nullability/required implementation, real simultaneous feed/finite delivery, finalizer tail bytes, simulator graceful delivery and reusable completed body executions. Internal controlled tests cover queued producers, admission-only sealing, no cancelation/interruption/cleanup election, already completed/canceled lifetimes, fresh generation state and a force-first/late-grace race. The combined HTTP/SSE/MCP graceful case now includes a fourth concurrent connection carrying a cooperating HTTP feed; the original finite drain, shared deadlines and forced-residual assertions remain. Detailed completed results are recorded in S8r above.

#### Approved and implemented — custom SSE transport access (F239)

The user approved making the existing package-private accessor on `HttpRequestResult` public and explicitly annotating its contained value as non-null. S8q implements the exact signature:

```java
@NonNull
public Optional<@NonNull SseHandshakeResult> getSseHandshakeResult();
```

This is the complete changed member signature. `SseHandshakeResult`, `Accepted.getClientInitializer()`, `Accepted.getClientContext()` and `SseUnicaster` are already public. No builder/copier setter needs to become public.

The accessor describes the **logical application handshake**, not proof that accepted headers were committed, initialization succeeded or broadcaster activation occurred. An empty optional is valid when request processing produced no SSE handshake result. A transport must use `getMarshaledResponse()` for the offered HTTP response and perform its framing/admission checks before commitment. For an actually accepted connection, it owns invoking the initializer once after the accepted response is written, keeping initialization bounded, preserving the client context and publishing queued catch-up writes/joining the broadcaster only after successful initialization. Initialization failure or shutdown must prevent activation and end the connection; establishment observation must precede termination observation even when initialization fails. Preserve the existing omission of handshake/context data from `HttpRequestResult.toString()`.

S8q verifies access with Java code in a package outside `com.soklet`, including accepted, rejected, absent and copied-result controls, and updates source Javadocs/migration/SSE SPI documentation together. The visibility change is implemented after user approval.

Preserve explicit nonempty protocol versions, supported revision subsets, the typed schema profile and current application-owned OAuth model. This review does not justify automatic protocol defaults, implicit legacy fallback URLs or an OAuth implementation inside Soklet.

### S10 — integration, then candidate validation

1. Verify each slice with targeted regression tests as it is implemented. Use JDK 17 and 21 for execution/backpressure changes, plus JDK 25 for current behavior.
2. Once the fixes and API decisions settle, run the core suite, static analysis, API compatibility/freezes, finite fuzz regressions and affected javax/jakarta/OTel/consumer suites. Compile website and source-doc snippets. Run builds serially with bounded heap/CPU.
3. Run targeted 2025 SDK/host interoperability and modern conformance after the relevant wire changes. Revisit Apps/Skills host qualification only where the changed code invalidates existing evidence; do not redo unchanged visual scenarios or the declined system-time-zone check.
4. Continue nightly fuzzing in CI. No repeated local 24-hour fuzz runs. Keep raw output/screenshots in CI artifacts or temporary local storage.
5. The user commits and pushes the final changes. **Then** refresh final downstream pins and run immutable candidate gates, including the opted-in Toy Store Docker smoke and official conformance, against those exact final commits/artifacts. Interim green CI is development evidence.
6. Respect the user's decision that Inspector's plain-403 OAuth bug does not itself block Soklet release. Continue to send correct denial/challenge behavior and retain an accurate upstream exception disposition; do not claim that an unpassed host scenario passed.

## Completed implementation slices

### S1a — paths, forms and charset parameters

**S1a: origin-form path preservation, direct form-pair parsing and Content-Type charset parameter parsing (F257/F258/F259).** Implemented on October 4, 2026:

- **F257:** shared URL parsing treats leading `/` input as a path, including `//x/admin`. Raw leading/repeated/trailing slashes are retained; the existing decoded normalization yields `/x/admin`, preserving every non-dot path component. Exact utility, Request and real HTTP route assertions cover this behavior, absolute URLs, query extraction, `OPTIONS *`, dot segments, NUL rejection, encoded-slash rejection and strict UTF-8. The formerly weak `AdvancedTests` path assertions now compare exact results.
- **F258:** raw pairs are split directly at `&` and the first `=`, then decoded with the selected query format and charset. Literal `#`, whitespace, quotes, braces, pipes and backslashes remain data; later fields are retained and validated. Full-map and fast single-name lookup agree without trimming raw data. Ordered immutable Lists, identical duplicates, bare/empty values, and existing annotation conversion are preserved. A single blank optional binds to `Optional.empty()`; repeated blanks fail scalar binding. Empty-name retention remains the separate proposed follow-up in S1.
- **F259:** charset parameters are found in any order using quote-aware semicolon splitting, case-insensitive parameter names and quoted-pair decoding. Multiple charset parameters are rejected with a fixed `IllegalRequestException`, including identical or invalid/empty duplicates; default HTTP handling returns a redacted 400. One unknown/invalid charset retains the documented fallback. Raw body bytes, form percent escapes and multipart field text honor a late selected charset with strict decoding.
- Updated Request/Utilities Javadocs, `MIGRATING_TO_4_0.md` and the website's request-handling documentation. No public types or signatures changed.

**Local verification:** 120 focused tests passed on JDK 17 and the same 120 passed on JDK 25, with zero failures, errors or skips. The JDK 21 `static-analysis` clean compilation gate also passed; the profile continues to emit advisory warnings. Coverage includes the new parsing regressions, existing strict-decoding/List/binding utilities, and small real-socket routing/form/charset checks. Builds ran serially with 512 MiB heaps and two active processors; no sustained fuzz/load run was started. Logs stay outside Git in `/private/tmp/soklet-remediation-s1a-20261004/`. This is development evidence, not final candidate validation.

**Historical F286 recheck after S1a:** absolute-form authority handling was then still incorrect. S1d subsequently fixed it and added the separate authority/trust-boundary regressions.

### S1b — physical header occurrences (F256)

Implemented on October 4, 2026:

- Removed the generic comma-splitting allowlist from physical header parsing. A field such as `Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate, br` now contributes one complete String value. Multiple field occurrences remain separate ordered immutable List entries, including identical repeats and empty fields.
- Normalized surrounding HTTP optional whitespace (SP/HTAB) consistently for physical, raw-URL/map, decoded-path/map and copied Requests. Other value characters, including nonbreaking spaces, remain data. Lazy single-name access and full materialization now agree; trace-context extraction uses the same occurrence model.
- Audited media negotiation, language ranges/locales, compression quality/wildcard handling, CORS request-header names, connection tokens, conditional validators, MCP language propagation and HTTP/SSE framing. Their field-specific parsing handles comma content. Regression coverage verifies that these consumers continue to work without generic splitting.
- Fixed MCP language-preference parsing to ignore empty physical fields between valid fields while retaining them in the raw Request map. First-occurrence weights and zero-weight exclusions remain intact. The 4,096-code-unit input bound counts ignored fields and separators before any combined string is built; existing malformed-input and expanded-range limits remain intact.
- An empty field remains visible to core accessors and scalar duplicate checks. One empty `getHeader()` value returns `Optional.of("")`; one blank annotated `Optional<T>` still binds empty, while repeated blanks or identical comma fields fail scalar binding. SSE body-header validation now sees an empty framing field instead of having it silently discarded.
- Both servlet bridges already delegate to the core header map. Added tests in Jakarta and javax for whole comma fields, repeated empties, case-insensitive enumeration, locale parsing and construction parity. Servlet `getHeader()` continues to return the first occurrence; its `getHeaders()` enumerates complete occurrences. No adapter production change or public signature change was needed.
- Updated Request/Utilities Javadocs, the migration guide, and website request/servlet documentation. The raw-header utility still unfolds obsolete folded lines for its existing utility callers; the live transport parsers continue to reject folding.

**Local verification:** 212 focused core tests passed on JDK 17; 222 passed on JDK 25, including ten selected live SSE/handshake cases. Jakarta's complete 231-test suite and javax's complete 219-test suite passed on JDK 17 against the core development JAR installed in a temporary repository. Runs had zero failures, errors or skips. The JDK 21 `static-analysis` clean compilation gate also passed, with existing advisory warnings. The new occurrence tests first reproduced the physical/map mismatch and dropped empty fields before the implementation changed; the MCP localization regression independently reproduced the loss of valid preferences beside empty fields. Builds ran serially with 512 MiB heaps and two active processors. Logs and the temporary dependency repository stay outside Git in `/private/tmp/soklet-remediation-s1b-20261004/`; no publication pins or candidate validation were produced.

**Historical follow-up:** HTTP/SSE framing was subsequently addressed by S1c; F286 and explicit empty names were addressed by S1d.

### S1c — bounded HTTP/SSE framing (F178/F179/F284/F205/F216)

Implemented on October 4–5, 2026:

- **F178:** the tokenizer caches its SPACE and CRLF delimiter searches at the current position. An incomplete delimiter search resumes at newly arrived bytes, retaining only the suffix that can straddle a read. The cache has two entries; movement, compaction and capture release preserve correct search state. The HTTP parser separately caps method tokens at 64 bytes and chunk-size lines, including extensions but excluding CRLF, at 8,192 bytes. Incomplete lines reject at the first over-limit byte; a split CRLF at the chunk-line limit remains valid. Capture regressions verify that a short malformed request line cannot borrow a separator or expose bytes from the following pipelined request.
- **F179:** HTTP/1.0 requests containing Transfer-Encoding fail as malformed framing before body parsing or resource-method dispatch. The response closes the connection, so a following pipelined request cannot run. Ordinary HTTP/1.0 Content-Length bodies and supported keep-alive parsing remain intact.
- **F284:** chunk sizes contain only hexadecimal digits. Extensions validate names and values against token/quoted-string syntax, including quoted pairs, supported SP/HTAB bad whitespace and obs-text inside quoted strings. Leading size whitespace, trailing size whitespace without an extension, invalid controls, bare CR/LF, unquoted obs-text and incomplete extension syntax reject. Valid extensions remain ignored when assembling the body. See [RFC 9112 chunk syntax](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9112.html#section-7.1.1).
- **F205:** SSE parsing preserves the raw input until validation and rejects any bare CR instead of manufacturing a new header line. CRLF and the existing LF-only input support remain. Whitespace-only header lines now reject as folded input instead of hiding later fields. SSE Content-Length uses unsigned ASCII decimal syntax and requires exactly one zero-valued occurrence; signed zero, comma values, repeats and all Transfer-Encoding fields reject.
- **F216:** rejected and ordinary finite SSE responses discard application framing and hop-by-hop fields, including fields nominated by Connection. The transport emits one Connection: close and the actual finite body length, preserving bodyless-status restrictions. Other fields, repeated authentication challenges, cookies and Date remain. Accepted streaming-handshake behavior is unchanged.
- Updated HttpServer/SseHandshakeResult Javadocs, the migration guide, and website request, server-configuration and SSE documentation. No public types or signatures changed.

**Local verification:** 114 focused tests passed on JDK 17 and 134 passed on JDK 25, with zero failures, errors or skips. Coverage includes parser fragmentation/compaction, boundary capture, valid/invalid chunk extensions, HTTP/1.0, physical headers, existing unparsed-request responses, real connection closure without pipeline dispatch, and 19 selected SSE handshake/header-limit cases. After the explicit Connection split-limit adjustment required by static analysis, 14 parser/SSE regressions passed again on JDK 17 and nine serializer/live HTTP/SSE checks passed on JDK 25. The JDK 21 `static-analysis` clean compilation gate passed, with existing advisory warnings. The new tests reproduced the reported framing defects before their fixes; an added capture-boundary regression also caught and corrected an issue in the initial method-limit implementation. Builds ran serially with 512 MiB heaps and two active processors; raw logs stay outside Git in `/private/tmp/soklet-remediation-s1c-20261004/`. No sustained fuzz/load run or release-candidate validation was performed.

**Historical next step after S1c:** authority/empty names were subsequently completed in S1d; file identity and cache metadata in S1e/S1f. Current next implementation work is the remaining transport disconnect/feedback review and MCP interoperability/session work described above.

## Finding dispositions

The following inventory accounts for all **201** main-catalog findings. “Fix” means recommended work after a reproducer/spec check against current source, **not** a claim that this assessment independently reproduced every entry. Related duplicates named by the report's “also reported as” clauses are covered with their primary finding.

- **Fix:** implement a bounded regression and correct the verified defect in the named slice.
- **Verify:** investigate the narrowed premise/contract, then fix or record a specific disposition; it cannot be silently counted as passed.
- **Docs:** correct/document the existing supported behavior in S9 or alongside the relevant slice.
- **API review:** show the exact proposed public signatures/semantics before implementation.
- **Defer:** deliberately outside this remediation package; current limitations remain explicit. Security/conformance evidence can promote an item later. These cover an extra stateless cancellation registry, optional public knobs/icons/skill features, idle-timer/store performance and extreme application-configured concurrency. Document limits and failure behavior now; this is not permission to leave a verified security defect unresolved.

Collector Javadocs and website distinguish lowercase MCP labels from uppercase native HTTP labels and explain the existing session-related causes without implying dedicated session metrics. |

### Appendix A/B treatment

The report's additional 39 deliberate/low-impact items and 12 rejected candidates are not automatically added to the release backlog.

- **Reopen F027 (S4)** and **F286 (S1)** as noted above; verify protocol obligations despite historical design decisions.
- **F235 (S8):** use a deterministic past-tick scheduling regression; if the timeout race is current, correct it while retaining request deadlines during drain.
- **F073:** optional removal of proven-unused provisional internal classes after checking production and test references. Preserve active channel/stream classes in the same package; package membership does not prove dead code.
- **F116:** inexpensive builder-validation consistency cleanup with S6 if the current mismatch reproduces.
- **F306/F307/F308:** cover active-session DELETE, a short real signal subprocess and aggregate multi-transport shutdown in the integration tests above. These are coverage improvements, not evidence of a present defect.
- **F004/F005:** retain explicit version sets and opt-in anonymous session allocation; improve configuration examples/diagnostics only where useful.
- Leave the remaining Appendix A/B deliberate boundaries intact unless new concrete evidence changes the disposition. In particular, preserve simulator capture limits, no implicit revision default, explicit Apps/typed-schema capability restrictions, numeric finite-underflow behavior and simulator service-isolation semantics. Do not turn optional feature omissions into a claim of full older-spec implementation.

## Completion standard

The remediation is complete when verified release defects are fixed, design questions have an explicit reviewed resolution, public docs match the implemented supported profiles, and targeted/integration tests cover the corrected paths. Deferred items remain clearly disclosed. Publication readiness additionally requires final immutable-candidate validation after the user's final commits; this plan cannot substitute for those results.
